A West Virginia clause was written instead, limited to soiling beyond reasonable wear and tear so it works under every reading (class decision 2), with the class decision 4 persons.
- (topic `carpet-cleaning`) Status Absent / Present in part: no carpet-specific rule exists (WVG-2006), but the deposit-use rules of W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(b)(2) and (b)(5) govern the deduction and turn in part on what the rental agreement provides, so the answer includes a clause (rule 27).
- (topic `casualty-and-mitigation-waivable`) Answered with an education row pointing to the existing clauses rather than a new clause: the statute that conditions a right on the lease (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28, 'unless the lease otherwise provides') is already used by `casualty-wv` limb (1) on topic `casualty-termination`, so the clause the topic needs exists (rule 27).
- (topic `casualty-and-mitigation-waivable`) W. Va. Code § 36-4-13 (a repair covenant does not bind a tenant without fault to rebuild after a fire or other destruction 'unless there be other words showing it to be the intent of the parties' (W. Va. Code § 36-4-13)) is cited by no existing WV row. It conditions a landlord-serving term on the lease's words, so under rule 54 it is recorded here as a declined option (shifting rebuilding cost to a residential tenant sits against the cautious course of class decision 5). No proposed row: it imposes no requirement on the landlord, and the library's surrender and tenant-damage clauses are consistent with it.
- (topic `certificate-of-occupancy-disclosure`) No clause: nothing turns on the rental agreement, and a lease statement about the certificate would be a voluntary disclosure that adds nothing to the landlord's position. The Fire Code's certificate duty runs to the 'owner or occupant' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subdivision 2.1.4.a); the row tells the landlord not to deliver possession before the certificate issues rather than shifting the duty to the tenant.
- (topic `cold-weather-vacate-notice`) Optional clause offered (rule 54): a cold-weather notice is lawful (no statute bars it) and serves the landlord by protecting plumbing; it is not a default. The Minnesota clause `cold-weather-vacate-notice-mn` rests on Minnesota's statute and is single-state, so it was not tagged (rule 26). The clause imposes no fee and leaves freeze damage to `tenant-caused-damage-wv`, so class decisions 3 and 4 are not engaged.
- (topic `common-area-lighting`) Tag of `common-area-lighting-ks` (KS;SC;UT;MI;NM) declined after reading its whole body. Basis check one way: it imposes no limit West Virginia lacks on what the tenant may be asked to pay. The other way: it has the landlord promise "Landlord shall maintain those and shall exercise reasonable care in the maintenance of the shared areas" with no arrears exception, which under W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(b) becomes a greater duty and gives away the W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(c) excuse; and its premise, that the tenant's electric service may lawfully carry shared-area lights, meets the PSC definition of residential service as the resident's own use (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-2, subdivisions 2.3.4.a-b). Declined on the second answer.
- (topic `common-area-lighting`) No West Virginia clause either (rule 54, declined option): a clause putting shared-area lights on the tenant's residential account rests on a reading of the PSC service classes that the rule does not support, and the library will not offer a term that could have a utility reclassify the tenant's account. The education row says the option is not offered and why, and that the landlord can write a clause with advice.
- (topic `communal-land-lease-approval`) GAPS item 13 calls this a territory topic needing a one-line confirmed absence; the body is one sentence plus the class decision 9 searched-sources sentence. No federal fetch is filed: West Virginia has no tribal trust land regime in the corpus, so nothing in the row turns on federal leasing rules.
- (topic `confirmed-absences-habitability`) Summary row cites this pass's batteries only (class decision 9); each absence is stated in full on its own topic's row, and this row names them in backticks rather than repeating their analysis (class decision 10).
- (topic `confirmed-absences-misc`) The summary covers the four subjects the other states' summary rows on this topic use (false application, detector shield, double letting, payment-method fees), each with this pass's own battery, and names other slices' passed batteries only for rows already answered (class decision 9). The detector shield is Present in West Virginia, so the row says so rather than listing it as an absence.
- (topic `confirmed-absences-misc`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `confirmed-absences-outside-title`) The summary covers the outside-title questions other states' summary rows use (squatters, cash receipts, registration, unregistered foreign entities) and the four GAPS item 10 subjects (foreign entity suits, assumed names, service on nonresidents, any-person reporting duties), each with this pass's own battery or a section read, and points to the WV row that carries each subject (class decision 10).
- (topic `confirmed-absences-outside-title`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `defective-drywall-disclosure`) Virginia's `defective-drywall-disclosure-va` clause rests on Virginia's own statute and is not tagged (rule 26). No West Virginia option is offered: a voluntary defect disclosure is covered by the cautious-course sentence and needs no clause of its own.
- (topic `deposit-surrender-notice`) No new clause: the lawful landlord-serving terms on this subject (the tenant's accurate-address duty, delivery by mail to the last known or forwarding address, and the six-month hold) are already in `security-deposit-return-wv`, and a refund condition tied to notice would run into W. Va. Code § 37-6A-4 (Claude's reading). The row is PROHIBITED because its operative advice is not to condition or forfeit the deposit on notice.
- (topic `designated-repairer`) Offered `designated-repairer-wv` as an optional clause (rule 54). The first pass marked this topic not applicable because Nevada's clause is tied to a repair-and-deduct statute (WV log §18); that was a topic disposition, not a WV log §6.1 declined option, so class decision 13 does not bar a consent-before-repair clause, which serves a West Virginia landlord precisely because no statutory self-help right exists.
- (topic `designated-repairer`) Variable request: other states use {{landlord_repair_contact}}, which is not in the brief's list of existing variables; the clause uses a bracketed prompt instead. The variable exists in the library (11 rows, including `designated-repairer-mn` and `designated-repairer-oh`), so the clause uses {{landlord_repair_contact}}.
- (topic `disaster-displaced-guests`) No clause: nothing turns on the rental agreement beyond the existing guest terms (`guest-policy`, `guest-policy-day-limit`, tagged WV), and every row on this topic in other states is a single-state education row resting on its own state's law, so none was screened for a tag.
- (topic `disaster-displaced-guests`) Legal call: whether an added charge for extra occupants of a month-to-month unit is part of the 'price' capped by W. Va. Code § 46A-6J-3(a) is not stated; the row takes the cautious course of treating it as covered (class decision 2).
- (topic `dv-deposit-timing`) Both topic questions answered no: the deposit clock does not wait for anything but the termination of the tenancy (W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(7)), and no section divides one deposit among co-tenants (W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(f) addresses assignees and sublessees only). The federal limb is a bounded claim over the federal sections held; no fetch is filed because the held federal victim-protection sections set no deposit timing and the row names that boundary and says other federal law may apply in assisted housing.
- (topic `dv-protection-order-chapter-moved`) Citation check done as the kickoff asked: every active WV row's protective-order, address-confidentiality, personal-safety-order and fair housing citations were extracted and checked against the current Code text; none cites a repealed or renumbered section, so no findings_existing is filed. The 2026 H.B. 4366 amendments to W. Va. Code §§ 48-27-209, 48-27-403, 48-28-4, 53-8-1 and 53-8-5 do not touch any subdivision those rows cite.
- (topic `dv-protection-order-chapter-moved`) The pre-2001 article numbers of the domestic violence law are not in the corpus, so the row tells landlords to update guides that predate the 2001 recodification without naming the old article.
- (topic `electric-submetering-disclosure`) Education only: there is no prescribed disclosure, and a resale clause cannot be offered while the electric resale rule stands unresolved for landlords (WV gaps log §4, class decision 2). Finding on `utility-billing-wv` filed on topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`.
- (topic `electric-submetering-disclosure`) The fetch is for the federal pipeline-safety definition of a master meter system (topic question 2: whether an apartment owner's gas master-meter piping is a regulated system). The definition was read at assembly and added to the row (WV gaps log §1).
- (topic `emergency-contact`) Tagged the shared optional `emergency-contact-co` (tenant names a contact) rather than writing a West Virginia clause, and wrote no landlord emergency-number clause: no statute requires one, and notice value alone is not a clause basis (CO-gaps 1); the education row recommends giving a repair number as practice. Kickoff item 2 asked for a confirmed absence on this key; WVG-5020 reruns the first pass's a-emergency-phone-r2 on this corpus.
- (topic `employee-screening`) No clause: screening staff is an employment matter and nothing turns on the rental agreement; a lease promise to screen staff would become a landlord duty. Other states' rows on this topic are single-state education rows, so none was screened for a tag.
- (topic `employee-screening`) The key-holder screening absence is also stated in this pass's education row on topic `key-control-policy`; this row answers the employee-screening key on its own battery and points there, so the two agree.
- (topic `energy-data-release`) Declined option: a tenant-consent clause would bind only the tenant, and nothing in West Virginia law makes a utility act on a lease term, so the education row says so and leaves the landlord free to add a clause with advice (rule 54).
- (topic `entity-landlord-court-representation`) Present. Magistrate court representation rests on a statute (W. Va. Code § 50-4-4a) and a court rule (Administrative Rules for the Magistrate Courts, Rule 10, court rule); the two are worded differently, and the body takes the cautious course (owner, officer, member, manager or regular employee with written authority, or a lawyer) rather than resolving which controls, since only case law could settle it (class decision 2).
- (topic `entity-landlord-court-representation`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened. One education row; no clause, because who may appear in court is not a lease term.
- (topic `environmental-event-termination`) Offered an optional mutual termination clause rather than declining (rule 54): no West Virginia statute bars a contractual exit, West Virginia has no just-cause rule, and W. Va. Code § 37-6-5 yields to 'special agreement' on notice. The clause excludes fire and casualty (left to `casualty-wv`), is unavailable where the landlord's breach caused the condition (class decision 5), keeps tenant-caused loss under `tenant-caused-damage-wv` with the class decision 4 persons, and creates no new charge (class decision 3). Iowa and Minnesota declined a similar clause because their statutes bar waiver of the repair duties; West Virginia's § 37-6-30 has no anti-waiver sentence, and the landlord-fault carve-out keeps the clause from operating as a waiver.
- (topic `environmental-event-termination`) Whether a contamination event or government order without physical destruction triggers the tenant's W. Va. Code § 37-6-28 surrender right ('destroyed by fire or otherwise') is case law, not searched; the education row takes the cautious course of treating the tenant's surrender as effective.
- (topic `eviction-penalty-clause-ban`) Declined option recorded in an education row (rule 54): an eviction charge is not barred by statute, but under class decision 3 it is an incidental default charge with no authorizing statute, so the library offers none and says the landlord may add their own clause only with advice. The topic question on the general penalty law is answered: no West Virginia statute voids penalties in leases of homes; the UCC rules are for goods.
- (topic `eviction-service-party`) Lease-named service agent declined as an option (rule 54) under class decision 2: W. Va. R. Civ. P. 4(d)(1)(C) recognizes agents 'authorized by appointment', but whether a lease term is such an appointment is case law not searched, and a failed appointment voids service; the row says the option exists, why it is not offered, and that the landlord may add their own clause with advice. No other state's clause tagged: the topic's clauses (`eviction-service-party-tn`, `eviction-service-party-mp`, `eviction-service-party-mn`) are single-state rows resting on their own statutes.
- (topic `eviction-service-party`) Kickoff item 2 settled from the corpus: the trial-court Rules of Civil Procedure are now held and Rule 4 was read whole.
- (topic `expedited-deposit-disposition`) Declined an optional expedited-processing fee clause (rule 54): W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(b)(5) would let a lease-named charge come out of the deposit, but no statute frames the service, its lawfulness turns on Claude's reading of W. Va. Code § 46A-2-128(d) (class decision 3), and the deadline is already an outer limit the landlord can beat for free. Recorded in the education row with the advice to use the landlord's own reviewed clause.
- (topic `family-child-care`) Education row plus an optional consent clause (rule 54): the lease decides, and a landlord who allows child care needs a term to set conditions. Every other state's child care clause is single-state and cites its own law, so none was tagged (BRIEF §3); the West Virginia clause copies the New York wording with West Virginia law named, uses the two builder variables that row already uses, and replaces its damage sentence with a pointer to the lease's existing damage terms so no lower fault standard enters (class decision 4). The Oregon clause rests on ORS 90.358 and was not screened.
- (topic `farmland-hunting-rights`) Agricultural tenancies stay out of scope (GAPS item 13; class decision 15); the answer says only what reaches a residential lease, from chapter 20. Tagged `hunting-fishing-consent-ok` (OK;NY), the simplest optional consent clause, rather than `farmland-sporting-rights-co` (exculpatory sentence) or `hunting-rights-ms` (adds a landlord crossing right that a West Virginia row would need to square with the lease's entry terms). The single-state clauses rest on their own states' statutes and were not screened.
- (topic `fee-unprovided-service`) Education row only; no statute conditions anything on lease text here. The CD-3 reading is applied cautiously: a lease-stated fee is part of the obligation, so the row rests the risk on the misrepresentation and deceptive-practices rules rather than on W. Va. Code § 46A-2-128(d) alone.
- (topic `forfeiture-redemption`) Pointer row keyed to topic `forfeiture-redemption` (rule 27), carrying a short summary of `edu-redemption-wv` and adding the W. Va. Code § 37-6-8 abandonment variant, which that row names only in its notes.
- (topic `frozen-standard-incorporation`) Answered as Present: the editions are fixed in the Fire Commission's rules, not in statute. The statute-only battery (WVG-8000) is deliberately bounded to the Code, Constitution and 2025 acts, and the body names that bound, because the rules plainly do name editions. A CSR-wide edition search (exploratory, not a battery) found named editions affecting buildings only in W. Va. Code R. §§ 87-1 and 87-4 among rules that reach an ordinary rental; the others are boiler, amusement, water-utility, licensing and similar rules.
- (topic `frozen-standard-incorporation`) The Fire Code's exemption for buildings used wholly as dwellings for no more than two families conflicts on its face with its own carbon monoxide subdivision listing one- and two-family rentals. The row does not resolve it; it says the detector statute applies to those homes either way (W. Va. Code § 15A-10-12(f)(3)).
- (topic `furnishings-included`) Tag declined for `furnishings-included-la` (BRIEF s3, rule 26): basis check (1) no limit beyond West Virginia law; (2) no right given away; no charge (class decision 3). But its sentence that Tenant "will return them at the end of this Lease in the same condition, except for normal wear and tear" shifts every non-wear loss to the tenant without a fault standard, and West Virginia's library rows shift loss only on the fault or reasonable-care standard of WV gaps log §4, class decision 4. So West Virginia gets its own clause, `furnishings-wv`, with that wording (rule 57: one clause and one education row).
- (topic `good-cause-notice`) The New York clause `good-cause-notice-ny` is a required disclosure under New York's statute and single-state, so it was not tagged (rule 26); West Virginia gets a confirmed-absence row with this pass's battery, replacing the first pass's background battery (WV log battery c-good-cause).
- (topic `governmental-fines`) Tags of `tenant-caused-fines-as` (17 states) and `tenant-caused-fines-oh` (4 states) declined after reading both bodies whole. Basis check one way (a limit West Virginia lacks): none; their carve-out for conditions the landlord is responsible for matches the class decision 5 cautious course. Basis check the other way (a right given away): none. The decline rests on class decision 4: both use "a member of Tenant's household, or a person on the Premises with Tenant's consent" and the trigger "caused or permitted", where West Virginia rows use the W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(a)(2) persons and a fault trigger; `tenant-caused-fines-as` also frames the payment as reimbursement rather than damages. The WV clause keeps the damages framing of `tenant-caused-fines-oh`, which works under every reading (class decision 2), with the West Virginia persons and a noncompliance trigger.
- (topic `governmental-fines`) Not a CD-13 matter: the first pass's declined option in `edu-government-fees-wv` was a reimbursement clause for municipal service fees (police, fire, garbage) billed by ordinance, not fines for violations the tenant side causes, so offering this clause does not reopen a §6.1 decision. Status Absent / Present in part: the statutory absence is confirmed, and the answer is a clause because the lease decides (rule 27).
- (topic `habitability-materiality`) Status Absent / Present in part: the private duty has no threshold, but materiality is present in the rent defense of W. Va. Code § 55-3A-3(b) and in the code-enforcement definitions. W. Va. Code § 37-15-7 uses 'materially affecting health or safety' (W. Va. Code § 37-15-7) only for factory-built home rental communities (class decision 15), so it is named in the notes, not the body.
- (topic `habitability-modifiable`) No new clause: the one lawful lease-side adjustment of a § 37-6-30 duty that serves the landlord, naming appliances not supplied under (a)(5), is already offered as `appliances-excluded-wv` (WV log §6.1, class decision 13). The Alaska water-supply question has no West Virginia lease analogue because (a)(7)'s limits are set by the statute's own facts.
- (topic `habitability-presumption`) Status Absent rather than Pointer: the first pass answered this topic in `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-wv` notes only, from a Code and Constitution search; this pass's whole-corpus battery confirms the absence, so the topic gets its own absence row.
- (topic `habitability-statement`) No clause offered: with no statement requirement there is nothing for a REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE clause to carry, and a lease statement of the duty would add nothing for the landlord while risking a greater duty under W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(b). The tagged `existing-condition` and `landlord-maintenance` already speak to delivery and repair.
- (topic `habitability-waiver`) The 'forego' limb was written as whole words (forego, forgo, forgoes) after an exploratory search showed 'forego\w*' matches 'foregoing' in hundreds of units; the logged battery WVG-1004 uses the whole-word form.
- (topic `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`) Item 3 (drug-laboratory rule): read W. Va. Code R. § 64-92, the filing effective May 1, 2022 (filed April 4, 2022; Word file; sunset August 1, 2027). The rule sets the content of the disclosure (location, remediation plans, related enforcement actions, certificate of remediation completion) and the persons (owner and any agent; any potential purchaser or occupant) but no form or deadline, so a lease disclosure clause is workable: proposed `meth-lab-disclosure-wv` on topic `meth-disclosure` (where WV already has `edu-meth-lab-wv`), with a finding on `edu-meth-lab-wv` updating its reason and its legal-watch reason. Consistent with rows/E's use of W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7 and W. Va. Code § 60A-11-5(a).
- (topic `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`) Found a statutory lease disclosure the first pass missed: W. Va. Code § 22-18-21(a) (hazardous waste storage, treatment or disposal, in the lease itself). It is this topic's clause; `edu-required-disclosures-wv` gets a finding. The environmental covenant copy duty (W. Va. Code § 22-22B-7(a)(3); W. Va. Code R. § 60-3-13, subdivision 13.5.c) runs to persons in possession but is not lease text, so it stays in the education row (rule 57 allows one new clause per topic); a separate covenant-incorporation clause like Massachusetts' and Connecticut's could be considered at sync.
- (topic `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`) The statute (W. Va. Code § 60A-11-5(a)) and the rule (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.1) differ on when a property may be reoccupied after a law-enforcement notice: certification only, or also test results at or below 1.0 µg/100 cm². The proposed clause follows the statute.
- (topic `health-district-rental-rules`) Status Absent / Present in part: the hand-out and referral duties are absent; what is present is the local board structure (W. Va. Code § 16-2-11) and nuisance powers (W. Va. Code § 16-3-6). The sewer-connection orders (W. Va. Code §§ 8-18-22, 16-13A-9) were seen in hits but left out of the row (one subject, rule 57).
- (topic `inspection-condemnation-disclosure`) Minnesota's and Wisconsin's disclosure rows rest on their own statutes and rules and are not tagged (rule 26). The drug-laboratory rule's 'related enforcement actions' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3) item is the only order-disclosure duty found; it is stated here and carried by the proposed `meth-lab-disclosure-wv`.
- (topic `inspection-notice-penalty`) Other states read this topic as the penalty for failing to offer or give notice of a move-in or move-out inspection (Minnesota, Wisconsin, New York, Washington); the first pass's line read it as an entry-notice penalty. The row answers both: no inspection-offer duty (WVG-5019) and no entry-notice statute (WVG-5004).
- (topic `key-control-policy`) No clause: nothing turns on the rental agreement, and a key-control promise in the lease would become a landlord duty the tenant could enforce. The shared `keys` clause, already tagged WV, carries the tenant's key receipt and return; the shared `lock-change-ak` clause is on another topic and was not screened here.
- (topic `key-control-policy`) `edu-no-security-device-rule-wv` says building, fire and local codes "were not read"; the Fire Code's own text sets no residential lock rule (only school classroom locking), so the row is not wrong and no finding is filed; its stale "not read" line is covered by the Title 87 legal-watch update.
- (topic `landlord-breach-remedy`) Status Absent / Present in part: the statutes give defenses, a counterclaim route and the casualty abatement, but no remedy scheme keyed to a landlord's breach of W. Va. Code § 37-6-30; the body overlaps `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-wv` only as far as needed to answer this topic's question and points there for the rest.
- (topic `landlord-liability-insurance`) No clause: nothing turns on the rental agreement, and a lease promise to carry insurance would give the tenant an enforceable term the law does not require.
- (topic `landlord-remedies-termination`) One education row; no clause. Possession, joinder of rent and money actions are statutory remedies the law implies into every tenancy, and the lease's `default-by-tenant-wv` already reserves them (CO-gaps 1). No acceleration clause is offered: no WV statute addresses it and the rest-of-term question is case law not searched (class decision 2).
- (topic `landlord-remedies-termination`) Every topic row is single-state (none shared), so no tag was screened.
- (topic `law-enforcement-cooperation`) The first pass marked this topic not applicable (WV log §18, d-child-coop). Re-answered from the corpus: the reporting duties binding any person required by GAPS item 10 are stated here, including the 2025 business-viewer duty in W. Va. Code § 61-8C-3c(d), which no existing WV row states.
- (topic `law-enforcement-cooperation`) One education row; no clause. Cooperation and reporting duties are not lease terms, and the optional `criminal-activity-wv` clause already covers criminal conduct as a breach. Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `lead-safe-certification`) The body of `edu-lead-abatement-wv` was checked for the words quoted in the finding; the finding quotes its body in double quotes as library text. The 10-day period comes from the rule; the statute leaves it to the director (W. Va. Code § 16-35-9).
- (topic `lead-state-notices`) The topic asks only whether state law adds notices beyond the federal disclosure; it does not. The row still states the federal steps in full because the WV tag segment on `lead-based-paint` predates the federal reading, and a landlord reading this topic needs the timing, retention and agent rules.
- (topic `lease-registration`) GAPS item 11: recording is never required for a lease term the library offers to be valid between the parties; it matters only against creditors and later buyers for a term over five years (W. Va. Code §§ 40-1-8, 40-1-9) and, by the words of W. Va. Code § 38-1-16(a), against a trustee's-sale buyer. One education row; no clause, since recording is the landlord's and tenant's own filing, not a lease term.
- (topic `lease-registration`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `lease-term-limitation`) GAPS item 11 answer for the lease terms the library offers: no spouse joinder and no recording is required for any lease term the library offers to be valid between landlord and tenant. A term over one year needs a signed writing (W. Va. Code § 36-1-3); a term over five years needs a deed and should be recorded to bind creditors and purchasers (W. Va. Code §§ 36-1-1, 40-1-8, 40-1-9). The one spousal rule is the notice duty of W. Va. Code § 43-1-2, enforced only in a later divorce; the optional acknowledgment clause gives the statute's own proof.
- (topic `lease-term-limitation`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `lease-type-parity`) No clause: nothing turns on the rental agreement; the optional `termination-notice-wv` already carries the special-agreement notice period. Other states' rows on this topic are single-state education rows on their own statutes, so none was screened for a tag.
- (topic `lease-type-size`) Pointer row on this key (rule 27): the type-size rule is already stated in `edu-plain-language-wv`; this row adds the bounded absence and the recording type rule of W. Va. Code § 39-1-11, which bears on the recording answer for GAPS item 11. Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `limitation-of-actions`) Kept consistent with `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-wv`: W. Va. Code § 55-2-6 applied to rent claims as Claude's reading, stated as generally ten years on a written lease the tenant signed and five on an oral lease.
- (topic `limitation-of-actions`) The two-year personal-action period of W. Va. Code § 55-2-12(a) is stated as the cautious course for property-damage claims because which period applies is case law not searched (class decision 2).
- (topic `limitation-of-actions`) Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened. One education row; no clause.
- (topic `liquidated-damages`) Pointed to `early-termination-ks` and the main pass's proposed "early-termination-wv" rather than redrafting an early-termination clause, as the launch instructions direct. Declined a liquidated-damages clause: the penalty doctrine is case law not searched, and under class decision 3 a default charge needs a statute that West Virginia does not supply.
- (topic `liquidated-damages`) WVG-11's term C (the phrase "liquidated damages") does not match W. Va. Code § 46-2A-504 ('may be liquidated in the lease agreement'); WVG-5008 searches the forms of "liquidat" and finds it. The section reaches only leases of goods, so WVG-11's residential-lease claim is unaffected.
- (topic `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`) Legal call: a lockout clause is treated as not lawful to offer, not as a lawful option declined. No statute bans dwelling lockouts in words, but W. Va. Code §§ 55-3-1 and 55-3-4 give a person 'forcibly or unlawfully turned out of the possession' possession and damages whatever their title, and the lawful routes are court process and the abandonment notice; whether a lease consent could make a lockout lawful is case law not searched, so the cautious course is no clause (class decision 2). The Texas opt-in clause rests on Texas's statute and is single-state, so it was not tagged.
- (topic `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`) Corrected before moving on: rule_type "PROHIBITED; CONSTRAINED" changed to "CONSTRAINED", because no statute bans dwelling lockouts in words; the limit comes from the court-process and forcible-entry sections (same type as `edu-self-help-eviction-wv`).
- (topic `maintenance-duty-shift`) No clause offered: whether a residential lease may shift W. Va. Code § 37-6-30 duties is unsettled (case law not searched), and the first pass declined the option (A-6), which class decision 13 keeps. The education row names the tagged routine-care clauses instead.
- (topic `meter-conservation-charge`) Answered from the Commission's rules now read, replacing the first pass's "Not applicable" line (WV log §18). No clause: no charge follows the meter, so there is nothing to disclose or allocate by statute.
- (topic `nonrefundable-deposit-separate-notice`) Status Absent / Present in part: the topic asks for a separate-notice rule, which West Virginia lacks; the written-agreement condition of W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(14) is present and already has a clause (`nonrefundable-fees-wv`, topic `nonrefundable-deposit-notice`), so no new clause is written here (rule 57; the topic needs only its own-key row, which points to the clause).
- (topic `notice-service-fee`) Kept as the first pass's declined option (class decision 13; kickoff item 2): the row says the option exists, why the library does not offer it, and that the landlord can add their own clause with advice (rule 54).
- (topic `notice-to-quit-waiver`) Kept the first pass's declined option (class decision 13; kickoff item 2): the row says the no-notice term exists under W. Va. Code § 37-6-5, why it is not offered, and that the landlord may add their own clause with advice (rule 54); the shortening option is already offered as `termination-notice-wv`, so no new clause.
- (topic `notice-to-quit-waiver`) The row says no notice is needed before filing for unpaid rent, so it names the federal CARES 30-day notice to vacate and points to `edu-cares-act-notice`, as the launch instructions require.
- (topic `notice-to-vacate-additional-terms`) No clause: a move-out form is not a lease term, and the conversion rule is already carried by `edu-condo-conversion-wv`; the row points there.
- (topic `ordnance-demolition-meter-disclosures`) California's combined clause rests on California statutes and is not tagged (rule 26). The shared-meter point is answered by the existing W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(17) rows; the Public Service Commission rules on resale and individual metering are this pass's utility topics and are pointed to in words, not repeated.
- (topic `other-landlord-facilities`) The steam boiler exception for 'boilers used in private residences which are used solely for residential purposes' (W. Va. Code § 21-3-7(a)) may or may not reach an apartment building's boiler; the row takes no position and sends the landlord to the Division of Labor (cautious course, class decision 2).
- (topic `other-landlord-facilities`) The elevator rule (W. Va. Code R. § 42-21) is OCR-only in the corpus; the row rests on the statute alone and paraphrases nothing from the rule.
- (topic `owner-move-in-reservation`) Wrote `owner-occupancy-termination-wv` with the shared `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` text rather than tagging it (changed after the independent check: its 30-day floor is a limit West Virginia law does not impose on an uncovered property, so rules 26-28 decline the tag; the floor stays as the cautious course under FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1)). Before that change the decision read: tagged the shared clause rather than writing a WV clause (rule 26): it is multi-state, rests on no other state's statute in its text, charges nothing, and both basis checks pass; single-state clauses on the topic (`owner-move-in-reservation-ca`, `owner-move-in-reservation-me`, `owner-recovery-ak`, `owner-occupancy-termination-hi`, `owner-occupancy-termination-nd` and others) were not screened for tagging. The first pass's "not applicable" line (WV log §18.2) rested on California's just-cause exception; the opt-in right is a separate question and is answered here.
- (topic `owner-move-in-reservation`) Legal call recorded: W. Va. Code § 37-6-11(b)(4) read as not reaching a landlord's reserved early termination of a fixed term (Claude's reading, as in WV log §6.3); the clause works under the broader reading too, because the notice it requires only protects the tenant.
- (topic `parking-rules-notice`) Offered an optional clause (rule 54; rule 27): W. Va. Code § 17C-14-13 makes removal turn on whether the vehicle is parked with the permission of the owner, tenant or lessee, and the lease is where that permission is defined. The clause adds advance written notice before a tenant's own vehicle is moved and excludes areas leased to the tenant alone, so it works under every reading of whose permission controls (class decision 2).
- (topic `parking-rules-notice`) No tag: the other states' parking-rules rows rest on their own statutes (Texas, Delaware, Connecticut and others); `parking-vehicle-rules` and `assigned-parking-space` are already tagged WV on other keys and their "in accordance with applicable law" and "subject to any limits applicable law places" hedges fit W. Va. Code § 17C-14-13.
- (topic `parking-rules-notice`) Consistency: the parking charge is treated as rent, as in this pass's answer on topic `unbundled-parking` (W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(11)); that row's new clause is referred to by topic, not id, because it is not yet in the library.
- (topic `part5-nonwaivable`) Status Pointer: `edu-prohibited-lease-terms-wv` already states every non-waiver rule this battery found that reaches an ordinary residential lease; the new row lists them briefly, adds the sale-leaseback answer and the habitability gap, and points there (rule 27).
- (topic `periodic-services-entry`) Tagged the shared optional `scheduled-services-entry-la`, which keeps advance notice and a knock before entry, rather than a no-notice clause: West Virginia has no rule that a conspicuous lease term permits no-notice entry, and case law on the tenant's possession was not searched (class decision 2). Its wording is a multi-state clause resting on no state's statute in its West Virginia use.
- (topic `pest-control-notice`) Stated the narrower rule (four rental units or less, ready-to-use products) as the cautious course where it and the statute's retail-product exemption (W. Va. Code § 19-16A-14(d)) diverge; the statute's exemption is tied to the for-hire license in W. Va. Code § 19-16A-7, while the rule's regulated-business category for apartment complexes rests on W. Va. Code § 19-16A-4(f)(1). Whether the rule validly reaches a landlord using retail products on more than four units is unsettled (case law not searched).
- (topic `pet-eviction-fact-sheet`) Absence row with a cautious course for animals left behind, pointing to the humane-officer reporting route (W. Va. Code §§ 7-10-4(a), 7-10-4a(a)) rather than the personal-property disposal options of W. Va. Code § 55-3A-3(h), because the statute speaks only of personal property and case law was not searched (class decision 2). No clause: no statute conditions a right on the lease here.
- (topic `plain-language-consumer-statement`) Education row on this key (rule 27); the first pass answered the topic inside `edu-plain-language-wv` (WV log §18; class decision 13), which stays the row for the rule itself. This row adds the bounded absences of the Pennsylvania-type features on a whole-corpus battery. The Pennsylvania clause rests on Pennsylvania's statute and was not screened for a tag; no West Virginia statement clause is offered because nothing requires one.
- (topic `portable-cooling-device`) Education only: the other states' rows rest on their own statutes (Washington, California) and are not tagged. Group Rules & Regulations because the practical point is the landlord's own window-unit rule.
- (topic `possession-bond`) Court rule against statute (rule 31): the appeal-bond figure stated is Magistrate Court Civil Rule 18(b)'s, with the statute's range named, as kickoff item 6 directs; the main pass confirmed the rule still reads as quoted, so no fix is proposed.
- (topic `private-well-testing`) Answered the Utah-style question (a landlord's own well becoming a regulated public water system) as present: West Virginia uses the same 15-connection or 25-person test (W. Va. Code § 16-1-2(18)). The federal monitoring and public-notice content is adopted by reference and not held, so the row states the duty and says the federal details are not covered.
- (topic `prohibited-acts-renter`) Tagged the multi-state `prohibited-acts-renter-wy` rather than writing a West Virginia clause: its covenant matches the damage ground of W. Va. Code § 55-3A-1(a)(3) and charges nothing. The single-state rows on this topic (North Carolina, Rhode Island, New Hampshire, Vermont, Delaware, Arkansas and others) state their own statutes and were not screened.
- (topic `promises-to-repair`) Offered `promised-repairs-wv` as an optional clause (rule 54): it serves the landlord by fixing the promises that W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(a)(5) and (b) would otherwise make provable from oral statements, and it was not among the first pass's declined options (WV log §6.1), so class decision 13 does not bar it. The New York model's written-modification sentence was dropped as unsafe under 'oral agreement' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(a)(5)).
- (topic `prop65-rental-warning`) California's Proposition 65 rows rest on California law and are not tagged (rule 26). The row names the West Virginia disclosures that do exist so a landlord does not read the absence as covering them.
- (topic `purpose-limitation`) Present, not absent: the first pass's background line read W. Va. Code § 61-8-5 as a crime only, but its subsection (c) voids leases of structures used for prostitution, lewdness or assignation on a party's conviction. No existing row states otherwise, so no finding is filed; the education row carries the rule.
- (topic `purpose-limitation`) No clause: the voiding is implied by statute into every lease (CO-gaps 1), and the existing `residential-use-only` (tagged WV) and `criminal-activity-wv` already give the landlord a lease remedy for other unlawful uses.
- (topic `purpose-limitation`) Legal call: whether a lease void under W. Va. Code § 61-8-5(c) leaves the occupant a 'tenant' for summary relief or requires another action is not stated; the row tells the landlord to use the courts and not self-help (class decision 2).
- (topic `recycling-notice`) Education row only. The Oregon clause rests on Oregon's statute and the California clause on California's statutes that authorize a source-separation term; neither was screened for a tag. A West Virginia lease may carry the landlord's own trash and recycling rules as ordinary rules (`edu-rules-regulations-wv`); no statute shapes such a clause, so none is offered.
- (topic `rent-demand-bar`) Education row only. The one shared clause on the topic (`rent-paid-without-deduction-wy`) is a single-state Wyoming row and was not screened for a tag (BRIEF §3). Status Absent / Present in part: no demand bar exists, but two statutes reduce or end rent (W. Va. Code §§ 37-6-28, 37-6-29), each yielding to the lease.
- (topic `rent-installments`) Tagged the shared optional clause: West Virginia has no installment or partial-payment statute, and the clause's late-charge sentence defers to the lease's own late-fee terms, which W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(b)(1) already requires. The weekly-period question was treated as case law (not searched) with the cautious course in the education row, rather than as a condition on the clause, because the clause keeps rent as a monthly amount.
- (topic `rent-receipt-anti-waiver`) Education row only: the topic is a uniform-act rule West Virginia lacks, and every row on the topic is a single-state education row, so there is nothing to tag. Status Absent rather than Present in part: the W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(e) successor rule is about deposits, not about collecting rent free of the habitability duty; it is named in the row as the closest rule.
- (topic `repair-cost-termination`) No new clause: the landlord's lawful lease-side option after casualty is already offered in `casualty-wv` item (3) (WV log §6.1; class decision 13). A general uneconomical-repair termination right outside casualty is not offered: it would sit against the uncapped repair duty of W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(a)(4), and whether a lease may create it is case law, not searched (class decision 2).
- (topic `repair-notice`) No separate notice clause: the tagged `landlord-maintenance` clause already asks for prompt written notice, and the repair contact belongs in the optional clause on topic `designated-repairer`, so a repair-notice clause would duplicate both (rule 57).
- (topic `rights-responsibilities-guide`) Education row on this key (rule 27). The first pass answered the related question of a state summary of rights in `edu-no-tenant-rights-statement-wv` on topic `tenant-rights-statement`; this row adds the guide-and-acknowledgment absence on a whole-corpus battery and points there. The Delaware clause rests on Delaware's statute and was not screened for a tag.
- (topic `sanitary-code-variance`) Used the water well siting variance as the illustration because it is the state health variance most likely to touch a rental (a private well serving tenants); the other variances found (child care, care facilities, zoning, mining, air and water quality) do not govern ordinary rental housing.
- (topic `seasonal-tenancy`) One education row; no clause. No West Virginia statute conditions a seasonal-tenancy right on the lease, and the fixed-term and holdover rules are already stated in `edu-holdover-wv` and `edu-termination-notice-wv`. Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `seasonal-tenancy`) Read W. Va. Code § 7-18-4 only as a battery hit; the row relies on W. Va. Code § 7-18-3 for the 30-day line, so the 2026 amendment to § 7-18-4 (H.B. 5065, not held) needs no fetch.
- (topic `security-deposit-nonwaiver`) Education row only: W. Va. Code § 37-6A-4 bars a term and conditions nothing on what the rental agreement says, so there is no clause to offer (rule 27). The body warns against a flat forfeiture as a cautious course because W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(b)(5) lets the lease supply other charges and the line between such a charge and a waiver is case law not searched (class decision 2).
- (topic `security-deposit-standards`) Education row only: no statute conditions deposit amounts on lease text, so there is no clause to offer. The fair-housing and assistance-animal limits are stated because they are the only rules on how deposits are set; the exemption detail stays in the notes.
- (topic `security-deposit-standards`) Drafting observation, no row changed: W. Va. Code § 16B-18-4(a) excepts from the owner-occupied rooming-house exemption 'other than subsection (b) of this section' (W. Va. Code § 16B-18-4(a)), a cross-reference that reads as a slip (the federal model excepts the advertising subsection). The row therefore states the exemptions only in general terms.
- (topic `senior-housing-work-card`) Education row only. The care-provider background-check program is named so a landlord that adds care services knows it crosses into a regulated business; its 2026 amendments (H.B. 4599) touch only the variance and clearance sections, which the row does not rely on, so no fetch is filed.
- (topic `sfr-occupancy-disclosure`) Education row only. The Nevada clause rests on Nevada's statute and was not screened for a tag; no West Virginia statute conditions a right on lease text here. The type-size question is answered by the new type-size row on topic `lease-type-size`, so this row points there and adds only the first-page and relative-font absence.
- (topic `sfr-occupancy-disclosure`) The code-affected list numbers the Stop Squatters Act's sections §§ 55-3D-1 to 55-3D-4, while the Code as served holds them at art. 55-3C (six sections). The row follows the Code as served (the corpus wins). No fetch filed: nothing in the row turns on the act's own text.
- (topic `shoreline-access-disclosure`) Education row only. No statute conditions anything on lease text; the Rhode Island clause rests on Rhode Island's statute and was not screened for a tag (BRIEF §3, other state's statute). A dock or water-use term is an ordinary lease term the landlord may add; no clause is offered because nothing in West Virginia law shapes it.
- (topic `showing-notice-waiver`) Tagged the shared optional `showing-notice-la` rather than writing a West Virginia clause: it is a multi-state clause resting on no state's statute, its brackets are the landlord's own choices, and West Virginia sets no entry notice (rule 26). Added an education row on the topic key, as the topic also asks whether a waiver must be in a separate document (no West Virginia rule requires one).
- (topic `single-family-zone-lease-limit`) Education row only; every other state's row on this topic is single-state and rests on its own law. Local zoning stays out of scope as GAPS item 2 directs; the row says so and names W. Va. Code § 8-1-5a(k) (class decision 12). The row does not say the accessory dwelling unit article bars a municipal owner-occupancy rule, because the text does not settle it.
- (topic `smart-access`) Education only; Washington's and California's rows rest on their own statutes. No clause: the library's `keys` clause and entry rows already carry the lease terms, and a smart-access clause would add drafting without a West Virginia legal hook.
- (topic `smoke-drift-waiver`) Declined a smoke-drift waiver (rule 54): no statute permits one, and the first pass's drafting call on exculpatory terms (WV log §6.3, class decision 13) keeps "not liable" sentences out of West Virginia clauses; the education row says the option exists, why it is not offered, and that a landlord may add one with advice. Offered a disclosure-only clause instead.
- (topic `smoke-drift-waiver`) The topic's workplace question is answered no: West Virginia has no general indoor smoking statute, and W. Va. Code § 21-3-8 reaches only posted factories, mercantile establishments, mills and workshops. County board of health clean indoor air rules, if any, are local and not held; flagged.
- (topic `social-security-defense`) Federal voucher rule named because it is the one held source that limits eviction for a late public payment; read from the held text of FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.310 and pointed to `edu-voucher-tenancy-addendum` rather than restated.
- (topic `special-district-representation`) Offered an optional clause because W. Va. Code § 36-4-10 makes a permanent local improvement assessment chargeable to the lessee only where the lease expressly provides (rule 27), and the tagged tax clause covers ad valorem taxes and insurance only.
- (topic `special-district-representation`) For the main pass: W. Va. Code § 36-4-10 governs the effect of a lessee's covenant 'to pay the taxes' (W. Va. Code § 36-4-10), which bears on the topic `tax-escalation` answer's statement that no West Virginia statute addresses passing property-tax increases to a tenant; it was not checked against that answer's battery here.
- (topic `sprinkler-disclosure`) No clause offered. The sign rule is a posting duty, not a term of the rental agreement, so the answer is an education row; a lease statement of sprinkler status serves the tenant more than the landlord, and the row says the landlord may add one if it matches the sign. The tenant duty not to disable fire protection equipment (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-6) is answered on topic `alarm-tampering-fee`.
- (topic `steam-radiator-covers`) No clause: no statute or rule conditions anything on the rental agreement; a cover-on-request promise would become a controlling lease duty under W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(b), so the row leaves it to the landlord's judgment.
- (topic `stove-refrigerator`) No clause: the tenant-supplied appliance case (Minnesota's clause on this topic) is already carried by `appliances-excluded-wv`, and the supplied-appliance list by the tagged `appliances-included`; the row points to both rather than adding a second clause.
- (topic `student-housing`) Education row only. The Ohio and Puerto Rico clauses rest on their own statutes and were not screened. The row adds the capacity caution (age 18, W. Va. Code § 2-3-1) and the familial-status caution because both bear on student leases; both state the cautious course where only case law could settle the point (class decision 2).
- (topic `subsidized-inspection-refusal`) Offered an optional West Virginia clause (rule 54): with no entry statute, access for an agency inspector exists only if the lease grants it, and the tagged `landlords-access` clause does not cover an agency inspector. The Colorado and Illinois clauses were not tagged: both are single-state rows resting on their own statutes, and the Illinois two-refusal eviction mechanism has no West Virginia counterpart and would sit badly with the federal termination grounds for voucher tenancies. The existing variable `inspection_notice_hours` was used rather than a bracket.
- (topic `subsidy-habitability-proration`) Education row only; all rows on the topic are single-state education rows.
- (topic `subsidy-late-fee`) Education row only; every row on the topic is a single-state education row. The body is bounded to the Housing Choice Voucher program, whose rules are held; other subsidy programs are named as not read rather than fetched, since the voucher rules carry the cautious course a landlord needs.
- (topic `tax-escalation`) Tagged the shared optional clause rather than writing a West Virginia clause: West Virginia has no statute on passing tax or insurance increases (its only statute, W. Va. Code § 36-4-10, sets the reach of a lessee's covenant to pay taxes and keeps improvement assessments and income tax off the tenant unless the lease expressly provides; the clause names what it shares; main-pass correction), the share is objective and tied to a stated bill, and the clause carries no default charge. The emergency price limit was weighed as a possible condition on lawfulness; it bounds any rent increase for a month-to-month tenancy at some times rather than making this clause unlawful, and the first pass's `rent-increase-midterm-wv` was handled the same way, so it is stated in the education row instead. Whether a tax or premium increase is a cost within W. Va. Code § 46A-6J-3(a)'s exception is Claude's reading, with the cautious course stated.
- (topic `tenant-bankruptcy`) Federal rows (class decision 14): written as a WV row from the held text of FEDERAL: 11 U.S.C. §§ 362 and 365, not as a shared federal row, because the topic's other rows are single-state and the WV-specific point is the post-judgment cure question under FEDERAL: 11 U.S.C. § 362(l)(1)(A) (W. Va. Code § 37-6-20, unsettled for summary relief). A shared federal tenant-bankruptcy row remains a candidate for the main pass (topic question 2).
- (topic `tenant-bankruptcy`) Court form SCA-M662NP (Affidavit: Notice of Filing of Bankruptcy Petition) appears only in the held magistrate forms index; its text is not held. The row does not rely on it; noted for the main pass in case the form should be saved.
- (topic `tenant-employee-housing`) Education row only, with the employment-conditional occupancy term recorded as a declined option (rule 54): whether ending a tenancy on the end of a job works under W. Va. Code § 37-6-5 and § 37-6-11(b)(4) is unsettled, and only case law could settle whether job-tied occupancy is a tenancy, so no clause can be drafted to work under every reading (class decision 2). Every other state's employee-housing clause is single-state and rests on its own law, so none was tagged.
- (topic `tenant-insurance-claims`) Declined a mutual waiver of insured losses (rule 54): no statute permits or regulates one, and for the tenant it releases claims against the landlord, which the first pass's exculpation call keeps out of West Virginia clauses (WV log §6.3; class decision 13). The education row says the option exists, why it is not offered and that the landlord may add one with advice.
- (topic `tenant-insurance-claims`) No tag: the other states' rows on this topic are single-state education rows on their own statutes (Colorado, Missouri, Michigan and others).
- (topic `tenant-portal`) Offered an optional portal clause because W. Va. Code § 39A-1-5(b) conditions electronic dealing on the parties' agreement (rule 27). It is drafted to match this pass's email and text notice clause on topic `actual-notice-method`: limited to documents no law requires on paper, carving out every default, eviction and cure notice (W. Va. Code § 39A-2-11(2)(B)), and leaving the tenant free to stop for future transactions (W. Va. Code § 39A-1-5(c)).
- (topic `tenant-portal`) Legal call: a fee for a portal the landlord makes mandatory is treated as an incidental charge under class decision 3, so the clause charges none and keeps a no-fee payment method (Claude's reading).
- (topic `tenant-portal`) Builder variables: used the existing "payment_portal_url" and "alternate_payment_method" (already used by `portal-rent-payment-ne`) and "landlord_notice_address" instead of bracketed prompts. Not tagged: `portal-rent-payment-ne`, `electronic-communications-and-portal-wy`, `tenant-portal-oh` and `tenant-portal-ut` are single-state rows resting on their own statutes (BRIEF §3).
- (topic `tenant-records`) One education row gathering the record duties and pointing to `edu-security-deposit-holding-wv`, `edu-senior-rent-statement-wv`, `edu-no-rent-receipt-rule-wv` and `edu-tenant-data-breach-wv`; no clause, because each duty is implied by statute and the only lease-adjacent option (a copy charge, as in Virginia) has no West Virginia basis and would be an incidental charge under class decision 3.
- (topic `tpa-exemption-notice`) Answered as a confirmed absence with this pass's battery WVG-5009 over the whole corpus (the first pass's c-rent-cap battery ran on its own export, Code and Constitution only).
- (topic `tpa-notice`) Other states read this topic two ways: the California rent-cap and just-cause lease notice, and a tenant's right to purchase on sale (Maryland, Iowa, Michigan, Washington). The row answers both: absent for the first (WVG-5009), absent but for the condominium-conversion offer for the second (WVG-5011).
- (topic `tpa-sunset`) The topic asks about a core tenant-protection statute on a dated repeal clock. No West Virginia statute has one; the row also names the three rental-related legislative rules whose own sunset dates the corpus shows, because a landlord relying on them needs the date.
- (topic `trespass-notice`) No battery: the row states present law read whole from art. 61-3B and art. 55-3C and makes no corpus-wide absence claim; the "no form" point is a reading of the sections themselves.
- (topic `trespass-notice`) Legal call: where the tenant invites a person the landlord has barred, the statute lets 'the owner, tenant, or the agent of the owner or tenant' (W. Va. Code § 61-3B-2(a)) request departure and does not rank them; the row advises barring a tenant's guest only from common areas, for a concrete reason, and with advice (class decision 2).
- (topic `truth-in-renting`) Education row only. The New Jersey and Michigan clauses rest on their own states' truth-in-renting statutes and were not screened for a tag; West Virginia has no counterpart, so no notice clause is offered.
- (topic `unbundled-parking`) Offered an optional West Virginia clause (rule 54) rather than tagging: every unbundled-parking clause in the library is a single-state row resting on its own state's law, and the not-rent wording several use conflicts with W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(11). The clause calls the charge rent, following the first pass's pet-rent call (WV log §6.3), so the landlord keeps the deposit and arrears remedies; that is Claude's reading. The existing variable "parking_fee_amount" was used rather than a bracket.
- (topic `usufructuary-lessor`) One education row; no clause. The life-tenant rule is implied into the tenancy by statute (CO-gaps 1); every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `utility-allowance-cap`) Declined option kept as the first pass decided (WV log §6.1, class decision 13), now with its own education row (rule 54). The shared `utility-allowance-cap-co` was screened for a tag and declined on basis check (2): its not-rent and no-late-fee sentences give up rent remedies under W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(11). The Public Service Commission rules were read for topic question 1; whether a rebilling landlord is a public utility under W. Va. Code § 24-1-2 depends on Commission decisions, not held, and is stated as unread rather than fetched.
- (topic `utility-deposit-return`) Present, so no battery: every statement is affirmative and quoted. The first pass's line called this topic not applicable because utility deposits are the utility's (WV log §18); the topic question is about the landlord's withholding list and rent definition, which West Virginia answers in W. Va. Code §§ 37-6A-1(11) and 37-6A-2(b)(3), so it gets a present row. `utility-deposit-identification-wy` rests on Wyoming law and is not tagged.
- (topic `utility-disclosure-attachment`) No clause: nothing to disclose by statute, and an optional utility-cost statement would add a representation the landlord must keep accurate without any right it secures; the library's utility clauses already allocate who pays. `water-power-availability-gu` rests on Guam law and is not tagged (BRIEF s3).
- (topic `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`) Status Present: the right exists on the customer side (W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-3-4.8.2.b.8, 150-4-4.8.2.b.8), so the answer is an optional clause with an education row (rule 54), as Alaska's shape; the owner-side absence is battery-backed. Whether a landlord is a 'responsible third party' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.8.2.b.8) is left to the utility, because the rules do not define it and Commission decisions are not held.
- (topic `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`) The posted-notice rules for landlord-held accounts belong to topics `utility-landlord-account` and `utility-shutoff-statute`, which West Virginia already answers; they are filed as findings on `edu-utility-landlord-account-wv` and `edu-utility-shutoff-wv`, not as new rows (rule 57, WV gaps log §4, class decision 10).
- (topic `utility-interruption-submeter`) No clause: there is no lawful interruption right to draft into a lease. The shared and other-state interruption clauses (for example `electric-submeter-interruption-tx`) rest on another state's statute and are not tagged (BRIEF s3).
- (topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`) No clause and no tag: there is no prescribed disclosure to draft, and the existing optional clause `utility-billing-wv` already carries the lease condition of W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(17); the other states' disclosure clauses rest on their own statutes (BRIEF s3). The resale rules are a utility-regulation limit on that clause, filed as a finding (kickoff item 3).
- (topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`) The new row `edu-utility-allowance-overage-wv` (topic `utility-allowance-cap`, this pass) says the Commission's master-meter provisions do not bar billing tenants and leaves the resale question to Commission decisions; it does not name the water rule's direct bar on customer resale, 'A utility customer may not resell or cause to be resold water' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-7-6.15.1), or the electric rule on furnishing for resale (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.14). For the main pass to reconcile at sync; this file does not edit it.
- (topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`) Statute and rule: W. Va. Code § 37-6A-1(17) allows submetering as between landlord and tenant if the lease so provides; the Commission's resale rules govern utility service. Read together, the statute does not exempt a landlord from the resale rules and the rules do not bar a lease term (Claude's reading; case law and Commission decisions not searched); the rows give the cautious course rather than resolving it.
- (topic `utility-transfer`) Offered a transfer-deadline clause and declined an opt-in cutoff clause: a cutoff is self-help with no statutory basis and collides with W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(a)(7) (case law not searched), so the education row says the option is not offered. The clause recovers the landlord's interim bills as loss from breach rather than as a rebilled charge, so it works under every reading of the Commission's resale rules (WV gaps log §4, class decision 2).
- (topic `utility-transfer`) W. Va. Code § 48-5-607 (divorce temporary relief) answers topic question 2 by analogy: a court can move an account mid-lease, but it is a divorce provision, not a protective-order one; the domestic-violence chapter has no utility-transfer provision in the hits read.
- (topic `veterans-incentive`) Education row only. The Nebraska concession clause rests on Nebraska law and was not screened for a tag; a West Virginia concession clause is not offered because no statute conditions anything on lease text and a discount is an ordinary rent term.
- (topic `written-notice-required`) One education row listing the written notices; no clause, because each writing rule is implied by statute into every tenancy and the shared `notices` clause (tagged WV) already defers to the law's method (CO-gaps 1). Every row on this topic in other states is single-state, so no tag was screened.
- (topic `written-notice-required`) Read the special-agreement sentence of W. Va. Code § 37-6-5 as reaching the notice period, not its form, and took the cautious course of written notice (class decisions 2 and 7).

### §5 Independent check

The check ran two rounds, the most the GAPS prompt allows, one agent at a time. Neither checker had written any of the answers. Each checker worked from the assembled delta, the answer files, the corpus and the SOP. Its findings were saved to a file and are summarized below. Every correction was applied at assembly and is listed in §4 under "Corrections made at assembly" (round 1: corrections 24 to 49; round 2: corrections 50 to 80).

**Round 1. Not clean: 16 findings.** By severity: 1 wrong-law, 1 unsupported, 7 inconsistent, 4 drafting, 3 minor.

What it covered:
- all 34 lease clauses: 22 new, 10 tagged and 2 proposed;
- all 113 absence topics, each tested against the terms of its final battery;
- a sample of 59 education rows (every third row, plus every REQUIRED or PROHIBITED row);
- seven cross-row themes;
- the scope fence, on all 175 delta rows.

The checker ran six bare-noun companion batteries (WVG-12000 to 12005). All passed, and none found a section bearing on an absence claim.

The main findings:
- **`pest-cooperation-wv` (wrong-law):** a flat promise to pay for all other treatment. That would bind under W. Va. Code § 37-6-30(b) and drop the arrears excuse in § 37-6-30(c).
- **The `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` tag (unsupported):** its basis check answered the wrong question.
- **`early-termination-wv`:** "only" shut out the Lease's other landlord exits.
- **`edu-no-ev-charging-requirements-wv`:** an unsupported sentence that contradicted the resale rows.
- **Five new rows:** each omitted the CARES Act 30-day notice to vacate.

**Round 2. Not clean: 19 findings, all fixed.** By severity: 2 wrong-law, 3 unsupported, 3 inconsistent, 4 drafting, 7 minor. All five serious findings were in rows the round-1 corrections had changed.

What it covered:
- each round-1 fix;
- the proposed `arbitration-agreement` tag segment, which round 1 had left out;
- all 26 proposed wordings in the findings on existing rows;
- a fresh sample of 52 new rows;
- the scope fence, for the changed rows.

It ran one bare-noun companion to WVG-1 (WVG-12100). That search passed and found no penalty statute besides W. Va. Code § 37-6A-4.

The main findings:
- **CARES sentences (wrong-law and unsupported):** two of them stated a written notice as federal law, though 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1) names no form. The five new CARES sentences also had no federal citation in their notes.
- **`edu-bed-bug-cooperation-wv`:** it still described the old pest clause.
- **The owner-occupancy tag:** after correction, it recorded a "yes" on basis check one, so the tag was withdrawn and the same text was written as `owner-occupancy-termination-wv`.
- **`meth-lab-disclosure-wv`:** moved to SERVES_LANDLORD and CONDITIONAL, because the rule sets no text, form or vehicle.
- **The arbitration segment:** rewritten with both basis checks, the charge screen and battery placeholders. It keeps the tag under Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule, though the clause's fee and opt-out terms are limits West Virginia law doesn't impose.
- **`environmental-event-termination-wv`:** now keeps any federal notice to vacate.
- **`tenant-caused-fines-wv`:** now uses the fault standard of class decision 4.

**Rows edited after the last round.** Round 2 was the last round, so every row below was changed after the last check and has not been checked again. Each change adopts the fix the round-2 checker proposed, or the narrower of its two options. The texts were checked against the statutes and passed the quote checker (0 failures):
- **Rows:**
  - new rows: `edu-bed-bug-cooperation-wv`, `edu-no-ev-charging-requirements-wv`, `edu-written-notice-required-wv`, `edu-no-lease-type-parity-wv`, `edu-no-good-cause-notice-wv`, `edu-no-possession-bond-wv`, `edu-no-seasonal-tenancy-rule-wv`, `edu-binding-arbitration-wv`, `edu-other-landlord-facilities-wv`, `edu-actual-notice-method-wv`, `edu-tenant-portal-wv`, `environmental-event-termination-wv`, `tenant-caused-fines-wv` and `edu-owner-move-in-reservation-wv`;
  - `owner-occupancy-termination-wv` (new in round 2);
  - the `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` tag (withdrawn).
- **Proposed rows:** `early-termination-wv` and `meth-lab-disclosure-wv`.
- **Proposed wording in the findings on existing rows:** `arbitration-agreement`, `edu-pool-safety-wv`, `edu-nonresident-owner-agent-wv` and `edu-servicemember-rights-wv`.

### §6 Findings on existing rows

1. `early-termination-ks` (GAPS item 7): GAPS item 7a: propose `early-termination-wv` (below, all fields) and remove WV from `early-termination-ks` at sync (states and its WV notes segment). The shared clause's fee of "one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater" (library clause text) grows with the remaining Term; the WV clause uses the flat one-month fee, keeps rent only to the termination date, applies only to a fixed Term and charges no fee on a statutory termination.
2. `tenant-caused-damage-wv` (GAPS item 7): GAPS item 7b edge. § 37-6-28 lets the tenant surrender and be 'relieved of all further liability for rent, after the time of such surrender' where the destruction was 'without fault or negligence on the part of the tenant' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28) and the landlord does not rebuild 'as soon as he can reasonably do so' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28). The clause charges lost Rent "up to the end of the Term" whenever "this Lease ends because of the damage" and the damage came from "Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, or any other person on the premises with Tenant's consent" (library clause text). Where a family member or guest caused the damage without the tenant's own fault and the tenant surrenders after the landlord's unreasonable delay, the clause would charge the rent the statute releases; whether 'on the part of the tenant' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28) reaches the tenant's household or guests is case law, not searched. Proposed added sentence, at the end: "Clause (2) does not apply after Tenant surrenders possession under West Virginia law because Landlord did not restore the property as soon as Landlord reasonably could." `casualty-wv` needs no change.
3. `default-by-tenant-wv` (GAPS item 7): GAPS item 7c. The clause says "Except for the posted notice applicable law requires when Tenant abandons the property while owing Rent, no notice or opportunity to cure is required before Landlord acts on a failure to pay Rent" (library clause text). On a covered property the lessor 'may not require the tenant to vacate the covered dwelling unit before the date that is 30 days after the date on which the lessor provides the tenant with a notice to vacate' (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1); `edu-cares-act-notice`). A lease term cannot displace that, but as written the sentence misstates what is required on a covered property. Proposed replacement opening: "Except for the posted notice applicable law requires when Tenant abandons the property while owing Rent, and any notice to vacate that federal law requires for the property, no notice or opportunity to cure is required before Landlord acts on a failure to pay Rent".
4. `edu-eviction-grounds-wv` (GAPS item 7): GAPS item 7c. The body says "No statute requires a notice or a chance to cure before you file on any of these grounds, in any kind of tenancy" and then lists "Two limits" (library row text), without the federal 30-day notice to vacate (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1); `edu-cares-act-notice`). Proposed: change "No statute" to "No West Virginia statute", change "Two limits" to "Three limits", and add: "and on a property with a federally backed mortgage or in a covered federal housing program, federal law may require 30 days' notice to vacate (see the CARES Act row)."
5. `edu-termination-notice-wv` (GAPS item 7): GAPS item 7c. The body says "No notice is needed from either side to end a lease whose term ends on a set date" (library row text), which is West Virginia's rule (W. Va. Code § 37-6-5, 'nor shall notice be necessary from or to a tenant whose term is to end at a certain time'). On a covered property the federal 30-day notice to vacate may apply (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1)); courts disagree on its reach (`edu-cares-act-notice`). Proposed added sentence after it: "On a property covered by the federal CARES Act, give at least 30 days' written notice to vacate before you require the tenant to leave (see that row)."
6. `edu-servicemember-rights-wv` (GAPS item 7): Its body says the federal Servicemembers Civil Relief Act is "not reviewed here" (library row text). This pass read 50 U.S.C. § 3955 (WV gaps log §1). Proposed replacement first sentence: "West Virginia has no separate state statute letting servicemembers end a residential lease; the federal Servicemembers Civil Relief Act does: a servicemember who signed the lease before entering service, or who later receives permanent-change-of-station orders or deployment orders for 90 days or more, may end it by written notice with a copy of the orders; for monthly rent the termination takes effect 30 days after the next rent due date, rent is prorated to that date, and you may not charge an early termination fee. The same right covers a lease signed on receipt of such orders when a stop-movement order then prevents occupancy, a spouse or dependent within one year after the servicemember dies in service, and a servicemember who suffers a catastrophic injury or illness in service; the tenant still owes rent prorated to the termination date and other lease charges such as excess wear, and you must refund rent paid in advance within 30 days." Further basis: FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(a)(3), (a)(4), (b)(1)(C), (e)(1), (f). Basis: 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)); 'effective 30 days after the first date on which the next rental payment is due and payable after the date on which the notice under subsection (c) is delivered' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(d)(1)(A)); covered leases (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(b)(1)); notice and orders (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(c)(1)(A)).
7. Proposed row `early-termination-wv` (GAPS item 7), every field:
   - id: early-termination-wv
   - group: Default & Termination
   - title: Early Termination
   - states: WV
   - supersedes: early-termination-ks
   - rule_type: RECOMMENDED
   - content_type: LEASE_CLAUSE
   - verification_status: VERIFIED
   - choice_group: (blank)
   - is_default: (blank)
   - topic_key: early-termination
   - lease_clause_basis: SERVES_LANDLORD
   - bodyText: If this Lease has a fixed Term, Tenant may end this Lease before the end of the Term by giving Landlord at least [number of days, no more than 30] days' written notice and paying Landlord, on or before the termination date stated in the notice, an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}). Tenant remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to that termination date. If Tenant gives the notice and pays the fee as this Section requires, Landlord accepts Tenant's surrender of the property as of the termination date, and Tenant will owe no Rent for any later period. This option does not apply to a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy, which ends on the notice West Virginia law and this Lease provide. Landlord may end this Lease early only as this Lease's other terms, including its Tenant Default terms, and West Virginia law allow. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including the right of a deceased Tenant's heir, personal representative, devisee or assignee to end this Lease (W. Va. Code § 37-6-11(b)), Tenant's right to surrender the property and be relieved of further Rent after a fire or other casualty (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28), the end of Rent when the whole property is taken by eminent domain (W. Va. Code § 37-6-29), and the rights of servicemembers under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act, which West Virginia extends to West Virginia National Guard members on state active duty (W. Va. Code § 15-1F-11). The early termination fee does not apply when Tenant's tenancy or liability for Rent ends under any of those rights, and Tenant owes no fee under this Section in that case.
   - notes: WV: Replaces `early-termination-ks` for West Virginia (GAPS item 7a; WV gaps log §6). Fee: a flat one month's Rent, the sum the library uses in `early-termination-wi`, `early-termination-ny` and `early-termination-ia`, in place of the shared clause's "whichever is greater" formula, which grows with the remaining Term and is the part most exposed to the penalty doctrine; whether West Virginia courts would treat any fixed fee as a penalty is case law, not searched (WV log §1.4). No West Virginia statute caps, sets or regulates an early-termination fee or liquidated damages in a residential lease WVG-11 (statute capping, setting or regulating an early-termination or lease-break fee, or liquidated damages, in a residential lease; search of WV Code, Constitution, Code of State Rules, court rules and forms, 2025 enrolled acts held in corpus-WV-20261120, 33305 units when run: term A 4 units (W. Va. Code § 24-2E-2; W. Va. Code § 31G-1A-2; W. Va. Code § 46A-6E-1; W. Va. Code R. § 106-19); term B 2 units (W. Va. Code § 19-4-17; W. Va. Code § 31G-2-17); term B& 6 units (W. Va. Code § 19-4-17; W. Va. Code § 31G-2-17; W. Va. Code R. § 106-19; W. Va. Code R. § 110-15; W. Va. Code R. § 157-2; W. Va. Code R. § 157-3); term C 32 units (listed in the battery log); control "zqxjvwk" 0; known positives passed: 2 real, 1 synthetic); the hits are telephone and broadband termination fees, assistive-device leases, reverse mortgages, a sales-tax rule on leases of goods, cooperative marketing contracts, highway contracts and the sale-of-goods liquidated-damages section. Charge reading: the fee is the price of an option the tenant chooses, so under the library's reading of W. Va. Code § 46A-2-128(d) it is part of the tenant's obligation, not a charge 'incidental to the principal obligation' (W. Va. Code § 46A-2-128(d)) that needs a statute (Claude's reading; WV log §6.3; WV gaps log §4, class decision 3), the reading the WV segment of `early-termination-ks` applied. Deposit: the fee can be applied from the deposit as one of the 'other damages or charges as provided in the rental agreement' (W. Va. Code § 37-6A-2(b)(5)); `security-deposit-use-wv`. Mitigation: the fee buys Landlord's advance agreement to accept surrender, so a tenant who uses the option owes no Rent after the termination date and no question of re-renting arises. A tenant who leaves without using the option is not charged the fee; that case stays under the abandonment and reletting statutes (W. Va. Code §§ 37-6-6, 37-6-7) and the default clause: the landlord 'may recover the rent owed up to the time when he or she became entitled to possession' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-6(a)), and with the reletting notice the tenant remains liable 'for the difference between the amount of rent received by the landlord from the new tenant, and the amount payable under the lease of the original tenant' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-7); a general duty to mitigate is case law, not searched (`edu-abandonment-mitigation-wv`, `default-by-tenant-wv`).
