No fee on statutory terminations (`edu-statutory-early-termination-wv`): death, where the right 'may not be waived by a lessor, lessee or lessee's heir, personal representative, devisee or assignee, by contract or otherwise' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-11(b)(4)) and the estate stays liable for 'The payment of rent or other sums owed prior to or during the two month written notice period' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-11(b)(3)(A)); casualty, where the tenant may 'surrender the possession of the premises and be relieved of all further liability for rent, after the time of such surrender' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-28; `casualty-wv`); a whole taking by eminent domain, where rent 'shall terminate unless the lease expressly provide otherwise' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-29), and the clause provides nothing otherwise; and the federal servicemember right, under which 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)), extended to a Guard member 'called to state active duty by the Governor for a period of thirty days or more' (W. Va. Code § 15-1F-11(b); `edu-servicemember-rights-wv`). West Virginia has no domestic-violence or illness exit (`edu-statutory-early-termination-wv`). Notice bracket: capped at 30 days, the figure the shared clause used and the first pass tagged, because 'Any lease provision or agreement requiring a longer notice period than that provided by this article, is void and unenforceable' (W. Va. Code § 37-6-11(b)(4)); its reach beyond the death subsection is unsettled (WV log §6.3; WV gaps log §4, class decision 7). The periodic-tenancy sentence fits W. Va. Code § 37-6-5 (`termination-notice-wv`). Landlord termination runs through the Lease's other terms, including `default-by-tenant-wv` (and, where used, `owner-occupancy-termination-wv`, `environmental-event-termination-wv` and `casualty-wv` item (3)); the shared clause's limb letting Landlord end the Lease if Tenant vacates "without notifying Landlord" is dropped, so abandonment stays under the posted one-month notice (W. Va. Code § 37-6-6(a); `edu-abandoned-property-wv`). rule_type RECOMMENDED (Taylor, 2026-11-12): no West Virginia statute requires the clause. At sync: add this row and remove WV from `early-termination-ks` (GAPS item 7a). Read section-open 2026-11-20 from corpus-WV-20261120; federal text saved from uscode.house.gov on 2026-11-20 (WV gaps log §1). Rule 15: written section-open.
8. `smoke-detectors-wv` (topic `alarm-tampering-fee`): Legal-watch reason update (kickoff item 3, last bullet). The notes say: "Outside one- and two-family dwellings no statute assigns the tenant a detector duty; the State Fire Code (legislative rule, not read) governs detectors there". The State Fire Code is now read: the filing is W. Va. Code R. § 87-1, Fire Code, filed May 13, 2026, Date Effective July 1, 2026, sunset August 1, 2031 (read in the filing's PDF text layer, not OCR). It adopts by reference 'NFPA 1 of the 2021 edition' and 'NFPA 101 2021 edition is adopted in full' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subsection 2.1 and subdivision 2.1.1), together with the publications NFPA 1 (2021) references (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subdivision 2.1.3); those model codes, where smoke alarm placement for apartment buildings would be found, are not held in the corpus. The rule does not apply to 'buildings used wholly as dwelling houses for no more than two families' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-1, subsection 1.6), so the statute alone governs the clause's one- and two-family case. The rule's own text adds no smoke-detector duty for landlords or tenants; its carbon monoxide subsection restates W. Va. Code § 15A-10-12(f)-(k) (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subsection 2.3). It adds one duty the clause's subject touches: 'No person shall render any portable or fixed fire extinguishing system or device or any fire warning system inoperative or inaccessible except as may be necessary during emergencies, maintenance, drills, or prescribed testing.' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-6, subsection 6.1), with notice to the local 911 center by the person responsible for maintenance or testing (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-6, subdivision 6.1.1); the clause's sentence "Tenant will not disable, remove or otherwise interfere with any detector" matches it, so the clause text needs no change. Fire alarm maintenance, repair or inspection for hire must be done by a registered company with a certified employee (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subdivision 2.4.1), which binds contractors, not the lease. The State Building Code filing (W. Va. Code R. § 87-4, Date Effective August 1, 2022, sunset August 1, 2027) adopts the 2018 International Residential and Building Codes for new construction where locally adopted (W. Va. Code R. § 87-4-4); not held. Replace "the State Fire Code (legislative rule, not read) governs detectors there" with "the State Fire Code (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1, filing effective July 1, 2026) governs there through NFPA 1 and NFPA 101, 2021 editions, adopted by reference and not held; the rule's own text adds no tenant detector duty beyond its bar on rendering fire warning systems inoperative (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-6), which the clause's tampering sentence matches". Legal-watch reason: the NFPA texts are not held, and the Fire Code filing sunsets August 1, 2031. Also replace "No fee for tampering: West Virginia has none, and the clause adds none." with "No fee for tampering: West Virginia has none, and the clause adds none; an optional actual-cost clause is `detector-tampering-cost-wv`."
9. `edu-alarm-duties-wv` (topic `alarm-tampering-fee`): The notes say "No tampering fee or tampering rule" and the body says building-wide rules come from "the State Fire Code and State Building Code, legislative rules not read here." A tampering rule exists in statute and rule: citations may issue for 'Tampering with, or rendering inoperable, except during necessary maintenance or repairs, on-premise firefighting equipment, fire detection equipment, and fire alarm systems.' (W. Va. Code § 15A-10-3(n)(5)); 'No person shall render any portable or fixed fire extinguishing system or device or any fire warning system inoperative or inaccessible except as may be necessary during emergencies, maintenance, drills, or prescribed testing.' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-6, subsection 6.1; filing effective July 1, 2026); and 'Any person who violates any fire and life safety rule of the State Fire Code is guilty of a misdemeanor' with a fine of $100 to $1,000 or up to 90 days in jail (W. Va. Code § 15A-10-3(q)). The Fire Code does not apply to 'buildings used wholly as dwelling houses for no more than two families' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-1, subsection 1.6). Replace the notes' "No tampering fee or tampering rule" with "No tampering fee; tampering with fire detection equipment is a citable Fire Code offense outside one- and two-family dwellings (`edu-alarm-tampering-wv`)", and replace the body's "legislative rules not read here" with "the State Fire Code adopts NFPA 1 and NFPA 101 (2021 editions), not read here, and itself bars anyone from disabling a fire warning system". This is separate from the finding already filed on this row about W. Va. Code § 15A-10-12(m) and (n), which is not repeated here.
10. `edu-no-lease-content-list-wv` (topic `association-obligations-disclosure`): The row says "West Virginia has no list of terms a residential lease must contain; a list exists only for factory-built home site rentals, which this library does not cover." (library text). For a unit in a condominium under the Unit Property Act, 'Every written instrument dealing with a unit shall specifically set forth the name by which the property is identified and the unit designation identifying the unit involved.' (W. Va. Code § 36A-4-5), and the first sentence of that section names leasing. Suggested change: add "A lease of a unit in a condominium under the Unit Property Act must state the name of the property and the unit designation (see `condominium-unit-identification-wv`)." Also, a lease of 20 years or more including renewal options of a unit in a common interest community triggers the resale-certificate delivery (W. Va. Code §§ 36B-1-103(25), 36B-4-109; `edu-association-disclosure-wv`); the row could point there.
11. `arbitration-agreement` (topic `binding-arbitration`): Proposed WV tag (GAPS item 8; Taylor's rule of 2026-11-07). West Virginia allows the clause: 'An agreement contained in a record to submit to arbitration any existing or subsequent controversy arising between the parties to the agreement is valid, enforceable and irrevocable except upon a ground that exists at law or in equity for the revocation of a contract' (W. Va. Code § 55-10-8(a)). No statute penalizes including or suing to enforce a prohibited term in a residential lease, other than the deposit-article penalty, which reaches only a waiver of art. 37-6A rights (W. Va. Code § 37-6A-4); the clause excludes deposit claims and waives no right. Basis check, both ways, and the charge screen are in the proposed segment (revised after the independent check; WV gaps log §5): the fee sentence in (d) and the opt-out in (e) are limits no West Virginia statute imposes, kept as the cautious course and offered under Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule; the clause gives away nothing the law gives the landlord. Basis check, give-away: it keeps possession actions and joined rent and damages claims in court, so the summary-relief route of W. Va. Charge screen (class decision 3): the clause charges the tenant nothing; the arbitrator may award only what a court could. Code § 50-2-1), so whether (b)(2) reaches a magistrate-court money claim is unsettled, and the clause works either way (`edu-binding-arbitration-wv`). 12. `edu-dispute-resolution-wv` (topic `binding-arbitration`): Its body says "This library does not offer an arbitration or mediation clause: it could delay the eviction hearing, which is held 5 to 10 judicial days after filing." If the proposed WV tag of `arbitration-agreement` is applied at sync, that sentence becomes stale for arbitration: the shared clause keeps "any action for possession of the Premises, including any claim for rent or damages joined with it" in court (library clause text). Proposed replacement sentence: "The library offers an optional arbitration clause for money disputes that keeps eviction cases in court (`edu-binding-arbitration-wv`); it does not offer a mediation clause."
13. `edu-alarm-duties-wv` (topic `confirmed-absences-misc`): The body states the detector duties and the misdemeanor fines but not the statute's civil-effect rules: 'A violation of this section may not be considered to constitute evidence of negligence or contributory negligence or comparative negligence in any civil action or proceeding for damages.' (W. Va. Code § 15A-10-12(m)) and 'A violation of this section may not constitute a defense in any civil action or proceeding involving any insurance policy.' (W. Va. Code § 15A-10-12(n)). The row's body ends its penalty sentence at "A violation is a misdemeanor (fines of $250, then $750, then $2,000), and a city or county may impose greater duties." and its notes cite (m) but not (n). Add after that sentence: "A violation may not be used as evidence of negligence in a civil damages case, and may not be a defense in a case involving an insurance policy." and cite (n) in the notes.
14. `edu-nonresident-owner-agent-wv` (topic `confirmed-absences-outside-title`): The body cites the LLC bar as "(W. Va. Code §§ 31B-10-1003(b), 31B-10-1008)" and says nothing of foreign limited partnerships. 'A foreign limited partnership transacting business in this state may not maintain any action, suit, or proceeding in any court of this state until it has registered in this state.' (W. Va. Code § 47-9-54(a)), and by transacting business without registration it appoints the Secretary of State as its agent for service (W. Va. Code § 47-9-54(d)). Add a sentence: "A foreign limited partnership transacting business here may not bring a suit in any court until it registers, though its leases stay valid and it may defend a suit; by doing business without registering it appoints the Secretary of State to accept service in cases arising from that business." and cite W. Va. Code § 47-9-54 in the notes.
15. `edu-applicable-codes-wv` (topic `frozen-standard-incorporation`): The body says ("The State Fire Code, a legislative rule of the State Fire Commission, has the force of law statewide as a minimum standard") and ends "none of these codes or rules was read here"; its notes say "Legislative rules (87 CSR) not read." The Fire Code filing effective July 1, 2026 (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1, filed May 13, 2026) is now read, and it says: 'This State Fire Code has no application to personal care homes caring for three or less patients or buildings used wholly as dwelling houses for no more than two families' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-1, subsection 1.6). It adopts NFPA 1 and NFPA 101, 2021 editions (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2). The State Building Code filing effective August 1, 2022 (W. Va. Code R. § 87-4) adopts the 2018 ICC codes and allows a local jurisdiction to reject the International Property Maintenance Code (W. Va. Code R. § 87-4-4, subdivision 4.1.e); the rule terminates August 1, 2027 (W. Va. Code R. § 87-4-1, subsection 1.5). Add after the Fire Code sentence: "It does not apply to buildings used wholly as dwellings for no more than two families, though the smoke and carbon monoxide detector statute still does." Replace "none of these codes or rules was read here" with "the Fire Commission's two rules were read (see `edu-code-editions-wv` for the editions they adopt); the model codes they adopt and local codes were not." Replace the notes' "Legislative rules (87 CSR) not read." with the two filings and their effective dates.
16. `edu-meth-lab-wv` (topic `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`): Item 3 (drug-laboratory rule), now read: W. Va. Code R. § 64-92, filed April 4, 2022, effective May 1, 2022, Word file of that filing, sunset August 1, 2027 (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-1, subsection 1.5). The row's body says the disclosure "rules are not covered here: before renting a property with that history, check the current rule and give the disclosure in writing before the tenant signs", and its notes say "The disclosure rule itself is a legislative rule, not read" and "No disclosure clause is offered (WV log §6.1): the content and form are set by the unread legislative rule." The rule sets the content: 'A residential property owner and any agent thereof shall disclose information regarding the identification of a clandestine drug laboratory on the residential property to any potential purchaser or occupant, including the location of the clandestine drug laboratory, remediation plans, related enforcement actions, and the issuance of any certificate of remediation completion by the department.' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3). It sets no form or deadline, so a written disclosure in the lease, given before the tenant signs, meets it; the clause is now workable and is proposed as `meth-lab-disclosure-wv` (topic `meth-disclosure`). The rule also adds owner duties the row does not state: on law-enforcement notice or awareness, keep the property 'unoccupied and secured until analytical test results indicate a level of contamination at or below 1.0 µg /100 cm2, a certificate of remediation completion is issued for the property by the department, or the property is properly demolished in compliance with 64CSR63 if applicable' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.1); 'Within 60 days of notice of the existence of a clandestine drug laboratory, test, commence remediation, or demolish the residential property in accordance with this rule and any other applicable law' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.2); in a hotel, motel or multi-unit building, secure, vacate and test adjacent units under the owner's control unless the commissioner allows occupancy behind a fire break wall (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.3); contract a licensed contractor within 60 days if tests exceed 1.0 µg/100 cm² (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.4); written delegation allowed (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.2). Suggested body change at sync: replace the sentence beginning "The statute also directs the Department" and the one after it with: "You, and any agent of yours, must also disclose the laboratory to any potential occupant, including where it was, the remediation plans, any related enforcement actions and whether the Department has issued a certificate of remediation completion; give this in writing before the tenant signs (see the Former Drug Laboratory Disclosure clause). Within 60 days of the notice you must test, begin remediation or demolish, and in a multi-unit building the units next to the laboratory must also be vacated and tested unless the Department allows otherwise. These rule duties are set to end on August 1, 2027 unless the rule is renewed." Replace the notes' "not read" sentences and the WV log §6.1 decline with the citations above and a pointer to `meth-lab-disclosure-wv`, `hazardous-waste-disclosure-wv` and `edu-contamination-disclosure-wv`. Rows already relying on this rule stay consistent: `environmental-event-termination-wv` and `edu-no-tpa-sunset-wv` cite W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7 and the August 1, 2027 sunset. Updated legal-watch reason for this row's notes: "Watch W. Va. Code R. § 64-92 (Clandestine Drug Laboratory Remediation, filing effective May 1, 2022), which terminates August 1, 2027 (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-1, subsection 1.5): on renewal or amendment, re-check the subsection 7.3 disclosure content, the subsection 7.1 owner duties and the 1.0 µg/100 cm² level; if it lapses, the disclosure duty and the clause's basis lapse while W. Va. Code § 60A-11-5 still requires the property to stay unoccupied until the Department certifies remediation."
17. `edu-required-disclosures-wv` (topic `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`): The body says "West Virginia requires few disclosures to residential tenants" and lists them, but omits the hazardous waste disclosure: 'any lessor in any lease or other instrument whereby any real property is let for a period of time shall disclose in such deed, lease or other instrument the fact that such property or the subsurface of such property, (whether or not the grantor or lessor is at the time of such conveyance or lease the owner of such subsurface) was used for the storage, treatment or disposal of hazardous waste' (W. Va. Code § 22-18-21(a)), limited to lessors who owned or had an interest at the time of that use or have actual knowledge of it. It also says "the Department of Health's rules govern disclosure of a former clandestine drug laboratory" without the content, now read (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3). Suggested addition at sync: "If the property or its subsurface was used to store, treat or dispose of hazardous waste and you owned it then or know of that use, the lease itself must say so (see `hazardous-waste-disclosure-wv`); a former drug laboratory must be disclosed to any potential occupant (see `meth-lab-disclosure-wv`); and a copy of any environmental covenant goes to persons in possession (see `edu-contamination-disclosure-wv`)."

   - topic_key: meth-disclosure
   - lease_clause_basis: SERVES_LANDLORD
   - bodyText: [Use only if a clandestine drug laboratory has been identified on the property, whether or not it has been remediated. Give a copy of this disclosure in writing to Tenant and to every other adult who will live at the property before Tenant signs. Do not let anyone occupy the property after a law-enforcement notice of the laboratory until the Department of Health certifies the remediation or the property has been demolished.] FORMER CLANDESTINE DRUG LABORATORY. Landlord discloses that a clandestine drug laboratory, meaning an area where controlled substances or their immediate precursors were manufactured, processed, cooked, disposed of or stored, or where someone attempted to do so, has been identified on the property. Location of the laboratory: [describe the unit, rooms, structures or other areas involved]. Remediation plans: [describe the remediation plan and its status, or that the affected structure was demolished]. Related enforcement actions: [describe any compliance order, cease and desist order or other enforcement action, or state that Landlord knows of none]. Certificate of remediation completion: [state that the Department of Health issued a certificate of remediation completion and its date, or that none has been issued]. Tenant acknowledges receiving this disclosure before signing this Lease.
   - notes: WV: Required disclosure: 'A residential property owner and any agent thereof shall disclose information regarding the identification of a clandestine drug laboratory on the residential property to any potential purchaser or occupant, including the location of the clandestine drug laboratory, remediation plans, related enforcement actions, and the issuance of any certificate of remediation completion by the department.' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3), the rule the statute calls for: 'Establishing requirements for property owners, sellers and landlords to disclose the existence of any former clandestine laboratory site or activity to any potential occupant of the residential property' (W. Va. Code § 60A-11-3(a)(6)). The four prompts track the rule's four listed items. Timing and form: the rule sets neither; 'any potential purchaser or occupant' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3) points to disclosure before occupancy, so the bracket requires it in writing before signing and to every adult occupant (Claude's reading; cautious course, WV gaps log §4, class decision 2). Who: the owner 'and any agent thereof' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subsection 7.3), so a managing agent must give it too. Scope: 'Residential property' includes any building primarily occupied as a dwelling 'that may be sold, leased, or rented for any length of time' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-2, subsection 2.18); the definition of a laboratory is the rule's (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-2, subsection 2.3; W. Va. Code § 60A-11-2(a)); the rule 'applies to all owners of residential properties which have been used as clandestine drug laboratories' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-1, subsection 1.6). The duty has no end date after remediation, so the clause applies to a remediated property too. Occupancy: the statute keeps the property unoccupied 'from the time the residential property owner is notified of the clandestine drug laboratory until such time as the department certifies that the completed remediation meets the numeric decontamination levels set forth in the legislative rule authorized in this article' (W. Va. Code § 60A-11-5(a)), while the rule also accepts test results at or below 1.0 µg/100 cm² (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7, subdivision 7.1.1); the bracket follows the statute, the stricter text (WV gaps log §4, class decision 2). Immunity after certified remediation for an owner who neither knew nor should have known (W. Va. Code § 60A-11-5(b), (c)) does not excuse the disclosure. Sunset: 'This rule shall terminate and have no further force or effect upon August 1, 2027.' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-1, subsection 1.5); after that date, unless the rule is renewed, the clause's basis lapses and it becomes optional good practice; legal watch on `edu-meth-lab-wv`. Consistent with this pass's environmental-event termination and repeal-date rows (topic `environmental-event-termination` and topic `tpa-sunset`), which cite W. Va. Code R. § 64-92-7 and the same end date. Education: `edu-meth-lab-wv`, `edu-contamination-disclosure-wv`. Filing read: W. Va. Code R. § 64-92, filed April 4, 2022, effective May 1, 2022, Word file of that filing (the PDF is a scan), not OCR. Currency: no 2025 or 2026 line names art. 60A-11. Read section-open 2026-11-20 from corpus-WV-20261120 (W. Va. Code §§ 60A-11-1 to 60A-11-6 read whole; W. Va. Code R. §§ 64-92-1, 64-92-2, 64-92-6, 64-92-7, 64-92-11, 64-92-14 read whole). Bucket (rule 55): the rule requires the disclosure, under W. Va. Code § 60A-11-3(a)(6), but sets no text, form or vehicle, so the clause is the landlord's written record of a disclosure the rule requires (SERVES_LANDLORD, rule_type CONDITIONAL, as `meth-disclosure-va`, `meth-disclosure-ca` and `meth-disclosure-sd`; WV gaps log §5). Rule 15: written section-open.
19. `edu-applicable-codes-wv` (topic `health-district-rental-rules`): Health part only (the fire and building code part is filed separately under topic `frozen-standard-incorporation`). The body says "local boards of health administer housing sanitation and may adopt local health rules with county or city approval" and ends "none of these codes or rules was read here"; the notes say legislative rules were not searched. The Title 64 rules are now read: the local board 'shall administer public health sanitation rules as specified by the State Health Officer with regard to:' a list that names no housing rule (W. Va. Code R. § 64-73-5, subdivision 5.1.3.b; filing effective July 10, 2026), and the general sanitation rule covers 'all institutions and schools, care facilities, lodging facilities, recreational facilities, and public restrooms' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-18-1, subsection 1.1; filing effective August 12, 2013), not ordinary rental housing. Suggested addition at sync: "No state health rule sets standards for ordinary rental housing; ask your local health department whether it has adopted local housing rules (see `edu-no-health-district-rental-rules-wv`)."
20. `edu-lead-abatement-wv` (topic `lead-safe-certification`): The notes say "federal law (42 U.S.C. 4852d, 40 C.F.R. part 745) not read" and "Legislative rules under § 16-35-4(1) not read", and the body says the owner "must notify the state before the project starts" without a period. Both are now read. The rule sets the period: 'Each owner or other person responsible for the operation of a building, facility, residence, or structure where a lead abatement project is to occur shall notify the division 10 days prior to commencement of the project and pay the notification fee set forth in Table B of this rule.' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-45-14, subsection 14.1; filing effective July 1, 2026, sunset August 1, 2031). The held federal text (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d; FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. §§ 745.100, 745.103, 745.107, 745.113) is now read. Suggested body change at sync: replace "the owner of a residence where a lead abatement project is to occur must notify the state before the project starts" with "the owner of a residence where a lead abatement project is to occur must notify the Department of Health 10 days before the project starts and pay its fee", and in the notes replace the two "not read" phrases with the citations above. Topics `lead-safe-certification` and `lead-state-notices` now have their own rows (`edu-no-lead-safe-certificate-wv`, `edu-no-lead-state-notices-wv`).

22. `edu-pool-safety-wv` (topic `other-landlord-facilities`): The body ends ("those rules and any local building code may apply to a pool at your rental property and are not covered here") and the notes say "Legislative rules (W. Va. Code R. title 64) and the State Building Code not read." The recreational water facility rule is now read (W. Va. Code R. § 64-16, filing effective 4/18/2007). It reaches 'facilities operated by communities, subdivisions, apartment complexes, condominiums' and excludes 'a private residential swimming pool as defined in this rule' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-16-2, subdivision 2.11a), which is a pool 'located at a single residential dwelling that houses no more than three families and that is used exclusively by the residents and their non-paying guests' (W. Va. Code R. § 64-16-2, subdivision 2.10); it requires a construction permit (W. Va. Code R. § 64-16-3, subsection 3.1) and an operating permit displayed at the pool (W. Va. Code R. § 64-16-4, subsections 4.1, 4.11). Replace the body's last sentence with: "If your rental is in an apartment complex or condominium, the pool is a recreational water facility under the state health rule, and a pool that serves more than three families or paying guests is likely to be one too: you need a construction permit to build or renovate it and an operating permit, displayed at the pool, to run it, and the rule's design and operation standards apply. A pool at a single home housing no more than three families, used only by residents and their non-paying guests, is outside that rule. Any local building code may also apply." The rule's barrier, signage and operation sections (W. Va. Code R. §§ 64-16-5 to 64-16-15) were not read for this finding; read them before the body states fencing or signage standards. `edu-other-landlord-facilities-wv` carries the permit sentence.
23. `edu-towing-wv` (topic `parking-rules-notice`): The row opens "West Virginia has no statute setting the signs, notice or fees for a private property owner who has a vehicle towed from a parking lot." and its notes say the only towing provision found is the highway-removal section. W. Va. Code § 17C-14-13 governs exactly this: 'It shall be unlawful for any driver of a vehicle to stop, park or leave standing unattended any vehicle on a private road or driveway or on private property without having express or implied permission from the owner, tenant or lessee of such land.' (W. Va. Code § 17C-14-13(a)); the owner, tenant or lessee 'may move, or have moved' such a vehicle without liability for the cost and without liability for damage unless negligent; 'The owner of such vehicle shall be responsible to the persons removing such vehicle for paying all removal costs. Any person who removes any vehicle under the provisions of this section shall notify the West Virginia state police of such action, and, if such vehicle is removed within a municipality, shall, in addition notify the police department of such municipality.' (W. Va. Code § 17C-14-13(c)). The row also says the Public Service Commission's rules were not searched; this pass read them: a 'Third-party tow' is a tow 'performed without the prior consent or prior authorization of the owner or operator of the towed motor vehicle' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-9-2, subdivision 2.4.27), only an authorized wrecker company may perform or charge for one (W. Va. Code R. § 150-9-6, subdivisions 6.1, 6.2.1), and it must mail an itemized invoice within 15 days (W. Va. Code R. § 150-9-6, subdivision 6.2.3). Suggested change: replace the first sentence with "West Virginia requires no towing signs at a private lot, but a vehicle parked on private property without the permission of the owner, tenant or lessee may be moved at the vehicle owner's cost; whoever removes it must notify the State Police and, inside a city or town, the municipal police, and the tow must be done by a wrecker company authorized by the Public Service Commission (see `edu-parking-rules-notice-wv`)." and drop the "Commission's rules were not searched" line from the notes. No other statement in the row was found wrong.
24. `edu-no-sprinkler-duty-wv` (topic `sprinkler-disclosure`): The body says the State Fire Code and State Building Code are "both are legislative rules not reviewed here" and the notes' boundary says legislative rules were not searched. The Fire Code filing effective July 1, 2026 (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1, filed May 13, 2026) is now read: 'Approved automatic sprinkler systems in accordance with the applicable NFPA Standard for Sprinkler Systems will be installed in all new buildings used for any occupancy except for Industrial occupancies' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-2, subdivision 2.2.1.a), and 'This State Fire Code has no application to personal care homes caring for three or less patients or buildings used wholly as dwelling houses for no more than two families' (W. Va. Code R. § 87-1-1, subsection 1.6). The rule's own text sets no retrofit duty for existing residential buildings (the existing-occupancy chapters of NFPA 101, 2021 edition, which it adopts, are not in the corpus). Replace "both are legislative rules not reviewed here" with "the State Fire Code requires automatic sprinklers in new buildings it covers (it does not cover buildings used wholly as dwellings for no more than two families), and its own text adds no retrofit duty for existing rentals; the national codes it adopts were not read", and point to `edu-sprinkler-signage-wv` for the posted-sign duty.
25. `edu-utility-landlord-account-wv` (topic `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`): The row says "no statute requires a utility to notify the owner before disconnecting a tenant's own service" and ends "Public Service Commission rules on termination of service were not read." The rules are now read. The electric and gas rules let a residential customer designate a responsible third party to receive all written termination notices and the personal-contact notice (W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-3-4.8.2.b.8, 150-4-4.8.2.b.8). Where the landlord is the customer of record of a master-metered multiple unit dwelling, or a non-resident customer of a single service location, the electric or gas utility must post Form 14-ME 'at least five (5) days prior to the scheduled termination' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.8.2.b.13; same in W. Va. Code R. § 150-4-4.8.2.b.13), and a water or sewer utility must post Water or Sewer Form No. 2 'at least five (5) days, excluding Saturdays, Sundays, and state or federal holidays, prior to the scheduled termination' (W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-7-6.8.1.h, 150-5-6.8.2.h). Suggested change at sync: replace the last sentence with "Under the Public Service Commission's rules, a tenant who holds an electric or gas account may name you to receive its shutoff notices (see `edu-utility-shutoff-notice-designee-wv`), and if an account in your name for a master-metered building, or for a single-unit rental you do not live in, is about to be shut off, the utility must post a notice at the property at least five days before (for water and sewer, five days not counting weekends and holidays)." and drop "legislative rules" from the notes' boundary sentence.
26. `edu-utility-shutoff-wv` (topic `utility-disconnection-notice-authorization`): The row says "No West Virginia statute sets notice or protections aimed at tenants before a utility disconnects service at a rental" and that the Commission's rules were "not reviewed here". The statute absence holds, but the rules now read give occupants notice when the landlord is the customer: Form 14-ME posted 'at least five (5) days prior to the scheduled termination' in a conspicuous common area and, 'Whenever possible', on each dwelling's main door (W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-3-4.8.2.b.13, 150-4-4.8.2.b.13), and Water or Sewer Form No. 2 posted five days, excluding weekends and holidays, before termination, which 'shall inform the occupant(s) of the date on or after which termination of service will occur and shall state the steps the occupant(s) can take to avoid termination of service' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-7-6.8.1.h). Gas Form 14-ME tells tenants they may 'apply for service in their own names becoming a new customer of the utility' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-4-11). Any customer gets mailed written notice 'postmarked at least ten (10) days prior to the scheduled termination' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.8.2.b.1) and personal-contact attempts, and electric and gas service may not be cut on a Friday, Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday or when the forecast is 32 degrees or colder (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.8.2.b.9). Suggested change at sync: replace "the Public Service Commission's rules, not reviewed here, govern disconnection by regulated utilities" with "the Public Service Commission's rules require a regulated utility to post a shutoff notice at the property at least five days ahead when the landlord holds the account, so tenants can act", and update the notes' boundary sentence.
27. `utility-billing-wv` (topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`): The clause offers submetering, energy allocation or ratio billing for any listed utility with the bracket example "[list each utility, for example water and sewer]", and its notes say "Not read: Public Service Commission rules and decisions on whether a landlord who resells or allocates utility service is regulated". The rules are now read: 'A utility customer may not resell or cause to be resold water furnished by a public utility on approved rates or contracts unless the customer is engaged in the business of distributing water as a public utility.' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-7-6.15.1), and 'Electric energy shall not be furnished by a public utility to any customer for resale, unless the customer is engaged in the business of distributing electric energy as a public utility.' (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.14). Whether submetered or ratio rebilling of water or electricity is resale is unsettled (Commission decisions not held), so under WV gaps log §4, class decision 2 the clause should work under every reading. Suggested change at sync: replace the opening bracket with "[Optional. Use only if Landlord bills Tenant for a utility. Do not use for water or electricity unless the Public Service Commission or a lawyer has confirmed that billing tenants separately is not a prohibited resale.]" and the example with "for example sewer"; update the notes to cite W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-7-6.15.1 and 150-3-4.14 and point to `edu-submetering-disclosure-wv` and `edu-electric-resale-wv`.
28. `edu-utility-apportionment-wv` (topic `utility-submetering-disclosure`): The row ends "Whether a landlord who bills tenants for utilities is regulated by the Public Service Commission depends on commission rules and decisions, which were not read." The rules are now read: the water rule bars a customer from reselling water unless it is in business as a public utility (W. Va. Code R. § 150-7-6.15.1); the electric rule bars a utility from furnishing electricity to a customer for resale (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-4.14) and says multi-unit residential dwellings 'should be individually metered rather than master metered' absent an exemption (W. Va. Code R. § 150-3-3.4.1); service furnished by an owner of a multiple apartment building to tenants as part of their monthly rent is classed commercial (W. Va. Code R. §§ 150-3-2.3.4.c, 150-7-3.25). Suggested replacement sentence: "The Public Service Commission's rules bar reselling water, and bar electric utilities from selling electricity for resale, unless the reseller is itself a public utility; whether billing tenants by submeter or formula counts as resale depends on Commission decisions, which were not read, so for water and electricity include the service in the rent or have tenants take it in their own names unless the Commission or a lawyer confirms otherwise." Notes: drop "PSC rules not read" and cite the rules.

### §7 Findings for other states

1. (topic `lead-state-notices`) Shared `lead-based-paint` clause (all states carrying it): no agent statement, which FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(5) requires when an agent leases on the lessor's behalf, and no reference to dated signatures (FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(6)). Suggested fix as in the WV finding.

### Proposed SOP changes

1. **Rule 19, plain term: search the statute's bare noun with no qualifier.** Battery WVG-3001 (topic `tax-escalation`) searched "property tax", "real estate tax" and "ad valorem tax" near a tenancy word, and missed W. Va. Code § 36-4-10. That section speaks of 'all taxes, levies, and assessments upon the demised premises' (W. Va. Code § 36-4-10). A later topic found it, and the answer was corrected (WVG-13; WV gaps log §4). Proposed addition to rule 19: every absence battery has one limb that searches the bare noun ("taxes", "assessments", "insurance") near a tenancy word, without the qualifiers the other states' rows use. Read its hits when there are 150 or fewer, and otherwise pair it with the statute's own phrasing ("pay the taxes").
2. **Rule 59 and the notes basis: a correction to a body sentence also corrects the notes.** In round 1 of the independent check, a CARES Act sentence was added to five bodies without the federal quotation in their notes, and two of them said "written" where 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1) names no form. Round 2 caught both (WV gaps log §5). Proposed addition: any correction that adds a legal statement to a body adds the controlling quotation to that row's notes in the same correction, and states a cautious course (here, written notice) as a course, not as the law.

### Proposed topic questions

None.

## Shared edit: lead-based paint agent's statement (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

From the West Virginia gaps sync (WV gaps log §6 item 21 and §7; backlog items from the Nevada and Puerto Rico syncs): the shared `lead-based-paint` clause gained a bracketed agent's statement and a sentence requiring each person who makes a statement to sign and date it, the two elements the federal rule requires in every lease of pre-1978 housing (40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(5)-(6); HUD's twin rule, 24 C.F.R. § 35.92(b)(5)-(6); both read on eCFR 2026-11-20). Uniform federal edit under rule 62: the same in every jurisdiction, no state term changed; it applies only where an agent acts for the landlord.

## Federal rows: the federal pass (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (backlog item 13; scope: private rentals and voucher tenancies, not federally assisted or tax-credit properties): nine federal education rows are tagged here and in every jurisdiction, each read from the official text (eCFR, govinfo.gov, the Federal Register; 109 quotations matched): `edu-scra-eviction-protections` (50 U.S.C. §§ 3951, 3931, 3958; the 2026 rent limit of $10,542.60), `edu-bankruptcy-eviction-federal` (11 U.S.C. § 362(a), (b)(22)-(23), (k), (l), (m)), `edu-fair-housing-coverage-federal` (42 U.S.C. §§ 3602(k), 3603(b), 3604(c), 3607(b)(1), 1982), `edu-fair-housing-harassment-federal` (24 C.F.R. §§ 100.600, 100.7; 42 U.S.C. § 3617), `edu-accessible-design-federal` (42 U.S.C. § 3604(f)(3)(C), (f)(4), (f)(7)), `edu-debt-collectors-federal` (15 U.S.C. § 1692a(5)-(6)), `edu-asbestos-demolition-renovation-federal` (40 C.F.R. §§ 61.141, 61.145), `edu-antenna-rights-federal` (47 C.F.R. § 1.4000) and `edu-voucher-repairs-and-rent` (24 C.F.R. §§ 982.404, 982.451, 982.453, 982.507, 982.510). A tenth, `edu-credit-reporting-furnisher-duties` (15 U.S.C. §§ 1681s-2, 1681i(a)(1); 12 C.F.R. §§ 1022.40-1022.43), was added the same day at Taylor's request. ## Model-law comparison: two new topics (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20: compare the library's topic list with the uniform model laws (the 1972 Uniform Residential Landlord and Tenant Act, the Revised URLTA of 2015, and UCC § 3-311) and answer any topic they raise that the library lacked. Two did: checks marked 'paid in full' (`paid-in-full-payments`) and a general duty of good faith (`good-faith-obligation`). This jurisdiction's rows are `edu-paid-in-full-checks-wv` (VERIFIED; W. Va. Code § 46-3-311; W. Va. Code § 46-1-308(b)) and `edu-no-good-faith-obligation-wv` (VERIFIED; Absence: W. Va. Code ch. 37). Each was researched from official text by an independent agent; every quotation was machine-checked against the saved page and the quotes are in each row's notes. 

### Declined option, 2026-11-21 (Taylor's decision)

- `edu-no-subordination-clause` tagged here and in every jurisdiction: the library offers no clause subordinating the lease to a mortgage (rule 54 row); the tenant's foreclosure protection is the federal PTFA (`edu-ptfa-foreclosure-tenants`).

## Lead-paint applicability (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Uniform federal label correction (rule 62): the shared `lead-based-paint` is now CONDITIONAL/REQUIRED (it was REQUIRED), since the federal disclosure applies only to housing built before 1978; no text changed. The app now adds it automatically, locked, when the property was built before 1978 or its year built isn't entered, and leaves it off for 1978 or later (Taylor's decision 2026-11-21).
